0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\hbox{\pagecolor{white}\vbox to5.39993pt{\vss\hbox to5.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}}\kern-5.39993pt\vrule height=5.39993pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 5.0pt\vrule height=5.39993pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-5.39993pt\hrule width=5.79987pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 5.0pt\hrule width=5.79987pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\hbox{\pagecolor{white}\vbox to5.39993pt{\vss\hbox to5.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}}\kern-5.39993pt\vrule height=5.39993pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 5.0pt\vrule height=5.39993pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-5.39993pt\hrule width=5.79987pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 5.0pt\hrule width=5.79987pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\hbox{\pagecolor{white}\vbox to5.39993pt{\vss\hbox to5.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}}\kern-5.39993pt\vrule height=5.39993pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 5.0pt\vrule height=5.39993pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-5.39993pt\hrule width=5.79987pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 5.0pt\hrule width=5.79987pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\hbox{\pagecolor{white}\vbox to5.39993pt{\vss\hbox to5.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}}\kern-5.39993pt\vrule height=5.39993pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 5.0pt\vrule height=5.39993pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-5.39993pt\hrule width=5.79987pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 5.0pt\hrule width=5.79987pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\hbox{\pagecolor{white}\vbox to5.39993pt{\vss\hbox to5.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}}\kern-5.39993pt\vrule height=5.39993pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 5.0pt\vrule height=5.39993pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-5.39993pt\hrule width=5.79987pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 5.0pt\hrule width=5.79987pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\hbox{\pagecolor{white}\vbox to5.39993pt{\vss\hbox to5.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}}\kern-5.39993pt\vrule height=5.39993pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 5.0pt\vrule height=5.39993pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-5.39993pt\hrule width=5.79987pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 5.0pt\hrule width=5.79987pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\hbox{\pagecolor{white}\vbox to5.39993pt{\vss\hbox to5.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}}\kern-5.39993pt\vrule height=5.39993pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 5.0pt\vrule height=5.39993pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-5.39993pt\hrule width=5.79987pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 5.0pt\hrule width=5.79987pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\hbox{\pagecolor{white}\vbox to5.39993pt{\vss\hbox to5.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}}\kern-5.39993pt\vrule height=5.39993pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 5.0pt\vrule height=5.39993pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-5.39993pt\hrule width=5.79987pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 5.0pt\hrule width=5.79987pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr}}\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}*(red)\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to5.39993pt{\vss\hbox to5.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-5.39993pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=5.39993pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 5.0pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=5.39993pt\kern 0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-5.39993pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 5.0pt\kern 0.39993pt}\nullfont\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\hbox{\pagecolor{red}\vbox to5.39993pt{\vss\hbox to5.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}}\kern-5.39993pt\vrule height=5.39993pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 5.0pt\vrule height=5.39993pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-5.39993pt\hrule width=5.79987pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 5.0pt\hrule width=5.79987pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\hbox{\pagecolor{red}\vbox to5.39993pt{\vss\hbox to5.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}}\kern-5.39993pt\vrule height=5.39993pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 5.0pt\vrule height=5.39993pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-5.39993pt\hrule width=5.79987pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 5.0pt\hrule width=5.79987pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\hbox{\pagecolor{red}\vbox to5.39993pt{\vss\hbox to5.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}}\kern-5.39993pt\vrule height=5.39993pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 5.0pt\vrule height=5.39993pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-5.39993pt\hrule width=5.79987pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 5.0pt\hrule width=5.79987pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr}}\kern 690.4pt}\kern 690.4pt}

Figure 10
:
Addition and subtraction of border strips.

The height
\mathrm{ht}(\gamma)
of a border strip is the number of rows plus one.
The formulas extend to
n<0
where

\lambda\pm[n]\quad\mapsto\lambda\mp[-n]\,,

(55)

and

C_{\alpha-\lambda}(q^{2m})=C_{\alpha}(q^{2m})-C_{\lambda}(q^{2m}).

(56)

Finally, we discuss the effect of framing change. A change of framing is a diffeomorphism
\Phi^{f}\colon S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\to S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
of the form

\Phi^{f}(e^{i\theta},u,v)\ =\ (e^{i\theta},u\cos f\theta,v\sin f\theta),

(57)

where
f\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. It acts on the torus at infinity as

\Phi^{f}(e^{i\theta},e^{i\phi})\ =\ (e^{i\theta},e^{i(f\theta+\phi)}).

(58)

Given an element
{\mathsf{Z}}
in the completed positive skein
\widehat{{\rm{Sk}}}_{+}(S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
,

{\mathsf{Z}}\ =\ \sum_{\lambda}H_{\lambda}(a,q)\,{\mathsf{W}}_{\lambda,\emptyset},

(59)

a change of framing acts as follows

\Phi^{f}({\mathsf{Z}})=\sum_{\lambda}((-1)^{|\lambda|}q^{\kappa(\lambda)})^{f}\,H_{\lambda}(a,q)\,{\mathsf{W}}_{\lambda,\emptyset}\,,

(60)

where

\kappa(\lambda)=\sum_{i}(\lambda_{i})^{2}-\sum_{j}(\lambda^{t}_{j})^{2}=2\sum_{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.31883pt\vbox{\kern 0.00002pt\kern 0.15941pt\hbox{\kern 0.31883pt\vbox to5.31883pt{\vss\hbox to5.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-5.31883pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt}\kern-0.15941pt\kern-5.31883pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt}\cr}}\kern 345.4pt}\in\lambda}c(\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to5.39993pt{\vss\hbox to5.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-5.39993pt\vrule height=5.39993pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 5.0pt\vrule height=5.39993pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-5.39993pt\hrule width=5.79987pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 5.0pt\hrule width=5.79987pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr}}\kern 345.4pt})\,.

(61)

The change of framing acts on the skein algebra
{\rm{Sk}}(T^{2})
simply as a coordinate change

\Phi^{f}({\mathsf{P}}_{i,j})\ =\ (-1)^{j\,f\,\sigma}{\mathsf{P}}_{i+j\cdot f,j},

(62)

where
\sigma=1
if the spin structure on
(0,1)
is bounding and
\sigma=0
if it is the Lie group spin structure.
Here we require that the 4-chain at infinity remains unchanged. This requirement leads to an intersection between the 4-chain in the new framing along a curve in the class
(f,0)
which then effectively scales the action of
{\mathsf{P}}_{i,j}
on
{\mathsf{Z}}
by
a_{L}^{-j\cdot f}
,

{\mathsf{P}}_{i,j}\to a_{L}^{-j\cdot f}\,{\mathsf{P}}_{i,j}\,.

(63)

Recall that in the context of (
60
) the solid torus
S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
fills the meridian of the torus at infinity, so we are required to use the bounding spin structure when applying (
62
).

5.3
The skein module of the solid torus with two boundary points

In this section we generalize the skein module in Section
5.2
and include also curves in the skein that are allowed to end at two fixed points in the boundary of the solid torus. In our applications these points will be Reeb chord endpoints.

We denote this skein module by
{\rm{Sk}}^{2}(S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
. It consists of formal linear combinations over
R
of framed links with open ends at two fixed boundary points of the solid torus up to framed isotopy and the skein relations (
28
). We fix an incoming and outgoing boundary point where the open component begins and ends. The precise position of the boundary points does not matter up to isomorphism as the boundary is connected.

The
R
-module
{\rm{Sk}}^{2}(S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
has a natural structure of an
R
-algebra as follows. Represent the solid torus as
A\times[0,1]
, where
A
is an annulus. Let the incoming and outgoing boundary points be
(p,0)
and
(p,1)
, respectively. Then the multiplication corresponds to gluing
A\times[0,1]
to
A\times[0,1]
by identifying
A\times\{1\}
in the first copy with
A\times\{0\}
in the second and rescaling the interval factor. The resulting algebra is commutative, see
[
38
]
.
Similarly,
{\rm{Sk}}^{2}(S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
also has the structure of an
{\rm{Sk}}(S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
-bimodule compatible with the product structures on
{\rm{Sk}}^{2}(S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
and
{\rm{Sk}}(S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
as follows. The maps

\displaystyle l\colon{\rm{Sk}}(S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})\otimes{\rm{Sk}}^{2}(S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})\ \to\ {\rm{Sk}}^{2}(S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}),

(64)

\displaystyle r\colon{\rm{Sk}}^{2}(S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})\otimes{\rm{Sk}}(S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})\ \to\ {\rm{Sk}}^{2}(S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}),

defined by placing a solid torus to the left, respectively to the right, of the solid torus with the boundary points are clearly homomorphisms of
R
-algebras.

For
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, let
{\mathsf{C}}^{\prime}_{n}
denote the arc from the boundary point at the top of the solid torus to the boundary point at the bottom which goes
n
times around the solid the torus.
Then
{\mathsf{C}}^{\prime}_{0}
is the unit of the algebra
{\rm{Sk}}^{2}(S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
and
{\mathsf{C}}^{\prime}_{m}{\mathsf{C}}^{\prime}_{n}={\mathsf{C}}^{\prime}_{n+m}
. Then, as an algebra over
{\rm{Sk}}(S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
with respect to either
l
or
r
,
{\rm{Sk}}^{2}(S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
is the algebra
{\rm{Sk}}(S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})[{\mathsf{C}}^{\prime}_{\pm 1}]
of Laurent polynomials in
{\mathsf{C}}^{\prime}_{1}
over
{\rm{Sk}}(S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
, see
[
17
]
.

Any element
A\in{\rm{Sk}}^{2}(S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
induces maps

l(\cdot,A),r(A,\cdot)\colon{\rm{Sk}}(S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})\ \to\ {\rm{Sk}}^{2}(S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}).

(65)

In the special case that
A={\mathsf{C}}_{0}
or
A={\mathsf{C}}_{1}
, we will write

\begin{split}&{\mathsf{P}}^{\prime}_{1,0}\coloneqq l(\cdot,{\mathsf{C}}_{0})\qquad{\mathsf{P}}^{\prime}_{0,0}\coloneqq r({\mathsf{C}}_{0},\cdot)\\
&{\mathsf{P}}^{\prime}_{1,1}\coloneqq l(\cdot,{\mathsf{C}}_{1})\qquad{\mathsf{P}}^{\prime}_{0,1}\coloneqq a_{L}^{-1}r({\mathsf{C}}_{1},\cdot).\end{split}

(66)

After choosing appropriate capping paths,
{\mathsf{P}}^{\prime}_{i,j}
is mapped to
{\mathsf{P}}_{i,j}
viewed as an operator on the skein of the solid torus.

6
Skein valued recursions – first examples

In this section we discuss skein valued recursions for the solid torus, the toric brane, the unknot conormal, and a neighborhood of a basic holomorphic annulus. The latter three were worked out in
[
7
,
22
]
. We mostly follow the description there but also add a new version of the recursion for the basic annulus that involves a certain power series.

6.1
The empty solid torus

In this section we consider the simplest case of the theory on the cotangent bundle of
T^{\ast}(S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
with no non-constant holomorphic curves. Let
L=S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
in the ambient space which is simply the cotangent bundle
T^{\ast}L
itself.
In order to obtain a recursion relation, we consider a version
W
of
T^{\ast}L
that is convex at infinity, with
L\subset X
being a Lagrangian with asymptotic Legendrian boundary torus
\Lambda\subset\partial W
.
Here
W
is
T^{\ast}S^{1}\times\mathbb{C}^{2}
with
L
equal to
S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
.
As in
[
22
]
we see that
\partial L
is
S^{1}\times U
, where
U
is the standard Legendrian unknot in the 3-ball.
This means that there is an
S^{1}
Bott-family of degree
1
Reeb chords. Morsifying this configuration, we find exactly one Reeb chord of degree
1
corresponding to the minimum in the Bott family. There are two holomorphic disks of the unknot that are asymptotic to this Reeb chord.
The corresponding recursion operator in the worldsheet skein with two boundary arcs is

{\mathsf{P}}_{0,0}^{\mathrm{ws}}-{\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}^{\mathrm{ws}},

(67)

which for an appropriate
4
-chain and after choosing one of the disks itself as capping surface gives the skein operator

{\mathsf{P}}_{0,0}-{\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}\ \in\ {\rm{Sk}}(L).

(68)

We obtain the skein recursion

({\mathsf{P}}_{0,0}-{\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}){\mathsf{Z}}_{L}=({\mathsf{P}}_{0,0}-{\mathsf{P}}_{1,0})1=0.

(69)

Here we used
W_{\emptyset,\emptyset}\equiv 1
to denote the empty link as an element in the skein.
The
U(1)
-recursion
1-\hat{y}=0
is obtained by specializing the above to the
U(1)
-skein where
{\mathsf{P}}_{0,0}\mapsto 1
and
{\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}\mapsto\hat{y}
. We point out that
{\mathsf{Z}}=1
in this case since there are no (bare) holomorphic curves.

6.2
The toric brane

Our next example is the toric brane in
\mathbb{C}^{3}
. This is a Lagrangian
L\approx S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
that supports a single holomorphic disk (and its branched covers), see
[
41
]
. The skein recursion operator for the toric brane was found in
[
7
]
, reading off the curves in the world sheet skein gives

{\mathsf{P}}_{0,0}^{\mathrm{ws}}-{\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}^{\mathrm{ws}}-{\mathsf{P}}_{0,1}^{\mathrm{ws}}\,.

(70)

which specializes to

{\mathsf{P}}^{\prime}_{0,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{\prime}_{1,0}-a_{L}{\mathsf{P}}^{\prime}_{0,1}\,.

(71)

in the open skein of
L
which for suitable capping disks gives

{\mathsf{P}}_{0,0}-{\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}-a_{L}{\mathsf{P}}_{0,1}\,.

(72)

Reducing to
U(1)
we get
1-\hat{y}-q\hat{x}
, which after the substitution
x\mapsto qx
gives the standard
1-\hat{y}-\hat{x}
.

We next discuss signs and framing factors in (
72
). As explained in
[
11
]
, signs on holomorphic curves depend on the choice of a spin structure on the Lagrangian. Here
L\approx S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
supports two distinct spin structures and (assuming the longitude generates
H_{1}(L)
), changing spin structure changes the sign of the last term in (
72
). In order to determine the remaining sign we use the representation of
\Lambda
as the front shown in
(
42
, Figure 9)
and observe, see
[
11
]
, that the two disks in question are copies of the disks on the standard 1-dimensional unknot with a stabilized boundary condition. Since for the standard unknot filled by a Lagrangian 2-disk these two disk form the boundary of a 1-dimensional moduli space it follows that their relative sign is negative for the bounding spin structure on the circle (here identified with the meridian). We conclude that also the first sign is as stated for spin structures on
\partial L
that extend to
L
. Finally, we consider framing data. We take a capping path close to the unknot disk. Then the second disk looks like a meridian and the third like a splicing of a longitude and a meridian in
\Lambda
which becomes an unknot in
L
. The
a_{L}
-factor comes from the kink in the last curve when viewed as a curve in the solid torus
L
.

We finally discuss this from the point of view of skein module homomorphisms over the augmentation variety, see Remark
4.3
. The augmentation variety has three asymptotic regions,
x\to 0
,
y\to 0
, and
x,y\to\infty
with constant ratio.
Each of these corresponds to a Lagrangian filling
L
of topology
S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
that fill in
{\mathsf{P}}_{0,1}
,
{\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}
, and
{\mathsf{P}}_{1,1}
, respectively and each gives homomorphisms

H_{\Lambda;\text{BRST}}\ \to\ {\rm{Sk}}(L),

where the homomorphisms take the empty word to
{\mathsf{Z}}_{L}
given by the partition function in
[
7
]
.

The recursion relation in generic framing can be obtained by transforming (
72
) according to (
62
) and (
63
)

({\mathsf{P}}_{0,0}-{\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}+(-1)^{1+f}a_{L}\,{\mathsf{P}}_{f,1})\,{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\mathrm{di}}^{(f)}=0\,,

(73)

where the specialization
a_{\Lambda}=a_{L}
was taken into account, since we take the operator to act on
{\rm{Sk}}(L)
.
This uniquely fixes the skein valued partition function of a framed toric brane
{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\mathrm{di}}^{(f)}

with initial conditions zero for all negative curves

{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\mathrm{di}}^{(f)}=\sum_{\lambda}((-1)^{|\lambda|}q^{\kappa(\lambda)})^{f}\left[\prod_{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.31883pt\vbox{\kern 0.00002pt\kern 0.15941pt\hbox{\kern 0.31883pt\vbox to5.31883pt{\vss\hbox to5.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-5.31883pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt}\kern-0.15941pt\kern-5.31883pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt}\cr}}\kern 345.4pt}\in\lambda}\frac{-q^{-c(\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.31883pt\vbox{\kern 0.00002pt\kern 0.15941pt\hbox{\kern 0.31883pt\vbox to5.31883pt{\vss\hbox to5.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-5.31883pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt}\kern-0.15941pt\kern-5.31883pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt}\cr}}\kern 345.4pt})}}{q^{h(\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.31883pt\vbox{\kern 0.00002pt\kern 0.15941pt\hbox{\kern 0.31883pt\vbox to5.31883pt{\vss\hbox to5.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-5.31883pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt}\kern-0.15941pt\kern-5.31883pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt}\cr}}\kern 345.4pt})}-q^{-h(\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.31883pt\vbox{\kern 0.00002pt\kern 0.15941pt\hbox{\kern 0.31883pt\vbox to5.31883pt{\vss\hbox to5.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-5.31883pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt}\kern-0.15941pt\kern-5.31883pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt}\cr}}\kern 345.4pt})}}\right]{\mathsf{W}}_{\lambda,\emptyset}\,.

(74)

We point out that the framing change formulas assumes that the 4-chain are unaffected by the framing change at infinity. This gives an intersection of the 4-chain and the Lagrangian along a curve in class
(0,f)
that shifts the action of the operator
{\mathsf{P}}_{0,1}
by multiplication by
a_{L}^{f}
.

6.3
The unknot

In this section we consider the skein recursion for various different Lagrangian fillings of the unknot Legendrian torus. In the limit
a\to 0
each of these give different fillings for the toric brane discussed earlier.
The augmentation variety of the unknot is

1-y-x+a^{2}xy=0.

(75)

We think of this as a plane curve and take the tropical limit, which means we consider the curve in
(\mathbb{C}^{\ast})^{2}\approx(\mathbb{R}\times S^{1})^{2}
and take the limit as the radius of
S^{1}
shrinks to
0
or equivalently draw
(\log_{t}|x|,\log_{t}|y|)
as
t\to\infty
. The resulting tropical curve is then subdivided into five regions that we label by the solid torus Lagrangian filling that gives the corresponding augmentation.

•

L_{l^{+}}
:
\{|x|<1,\ |y|=1\}

•

L_{m^{+}}
:
\{|x|=1,\ |y|<1\}

•

L_{l^{-}}
:
\{|x|>a^{2},\ |y|=a^{2}\}

•

L_{m^{-}}
:
\{|x|=a^{2},\ |y|>a^{2}\}

•

L_{d}
:
\{1<|x|=|y|<a^{2}\}
.

We discuss skein valued curve counts and recursions for three of them, since the remaining two are related by a simple change of variables.

We start with the generator of the world sheet skein
D
-module.
The ideal boundary of the unknot conormal Lagrangian
L_{U}
is a Legendrian torus
\Lambda_{U}\subset U^{\ast}S^{3}\approx S^{3}\times S^{2}
which can be thought of as the ideal contact boundary of either
T^{\ast}S^{3}
or the resolved conifold
\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}(-1)\oplus\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}(-1)
. The Legendrian
\Lambda_{U}
has a single Reeb chord
c
of degree one and no Reeb chords of degree zero or lower. This means that the recursion relation in the worldsheet skein can be read off directly from the curves at infinity that asymptotes to the Reeb chord
c
. These curves were determined using Morse flow trees in
[
11
]
and the resulting curves are shown in Figure
11
: there are four rigid disks with a positive puncture at
c
. We get the ideal generator in the worldsheet skein

{\mathsf{A}}_{U}={\mathsf{P}}_{0,0}^{\mathrm{ws}}-{\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}^{\mathrm{ws}}-{\mathsf{P}}_{0,1}^{\mathrm{ws}}+{\mathsf{P}}_{1,1}^{\mathrm{ws}}.

(76)

Figure 11
:
Left: The Legendrian torus of the unknot. Four disks boundaries are marked in solid green, a choice of capping path by a dashed line. Homology classes of boundary curves with this choice of capping are indicated in units of meridian and longitude windings
(\mu,\lambda)
. Right: joining the capping path to each of the disk boundaries. Joining to
\gamma_{01}
introduces a positive curl, which is responsible for the power of
a_{L}
in (
77
).

6.3.1
L_{l^{\pm}}
– the conormal filling

The skein valued recursion relation and its solution for the unknot conormal filling
L_{l^{\pm}}
were first worked out in
[
7
]
. Note that the sign in
l^{\pm}
corresponds to the direction of the shift of the conormal. The orientation of the basic annulus changes according to the shift, the unknot in
S^{3}
does not change but the direction of the curves in the conormal does. It is easy to find the relevant change of variables and we therefore focus on on case
L_{l^{+}}
, say.

In order to write a skein recursion with operators in
{\rm{Sk}}(\Lambda_{U})
we pick a capping disk to close the disks off. With the boundary of that capping disk
\eta
in Figure
11
we get the following recursion:
It was shown in
[
7
]
that the count of curves at infinity in the skein uplifts to the following operator in the skein of
\Lambda_{U}
for a certain choice of 4-chains and spin structures:

{\mathsf{A}}_{U}={\mathsf{A}}_{U;\lambda}={\mathsf{P}}_{0,0}-{\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}-a^{-1}a_{L_{\lambda}}{\mathsf{P}}_{0,1}+a{\mathsf{P}}_{1,1}.

(77)

The classes of the curves are evident from the picture. We discuss signs and framing factors.

The signs in the formula depend on a spin structure. The particular signs here correspond to the spin structure on the boundary torus that restrict to the bounding spin structure on both the meridian and the longitude circles. The sign follows from index bundle considerations, see
(
11
, Theorem 6.4)
(where the calculation is carried out in the Lie group spin structure on
\Lambda_{U}
is used).

Consider next powers of
a
. In
[
11
]
intersections of the four disks where determined relative a specific dual
4
-chain in
T^{\ast}S^{3}
of the fiber sphere in
U^{\ast}S^{3}
. For the skein valued count we use that 4-chain together with its image under fiber reflection in
T^{\ast}S^{3}
. The result there would then correspond, in our current notation to coefficient
a^{0}
for
{\mathsf{P}}_{0,0}
,
{\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}
,
{\mathsf{P}}_{0,1}
, and
a^{2}
for
{\mathsf{P}}_{1,1}
. Here we must consider also intersections with the negative 4-chain near the south pole of the fiber sphere. For the curves bounded by
{\mathsf{P}}_{0,0}
and
{\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}
there are no intersections, the curve
{\mathsf{P}}_{1,1}
has one intersection which now gives
a^{1}
(formerly this intersection gave
a^{2}
), and the curve
{\mathsf{P}}_{0,1}
has a negative intersection near the south pole which gives the coefficient
a^{-1}
. (To see the negative sign, note that the linear combination of curves with boundary
{\mathsf{P}}_{1,1}-{\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}-{\mathsf{P}}_{0,1}
represents a cycle that wraps once around the generator of
H^{2}(U^{\ast}S^{3})
corresponding to
a^{2}
.)

Finally, the power of
a_{L_{l^{+}}}
in front of
{\mathsf{P}}_{0,1}
arises as the corresponding factor in (
72
) (note that
{\mathsf{P}}_{1,1}
need not be related to any curve in the solid torus).

The skein recursion operator (
77
) reduces to the
U(1)
recursion operator when specializing
N=1
, which corresponds to the following replacements

a_{\Lambda_{U}}\to q\,,\qquad{\mathsf{P}}_{0,0}\to 1\,,\qquad{\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}\to\hat{y}\,,\qquad{\mathsf{P}}_{0,1}\to\hat{x}\,,\qquad{\mathsf{P}}_{1,1}\to-q\hat{x}\hat{y}\,.

(78)

Also, the recursion operator in generic framing can be obtained by transforming (
77
) according to (
62
)

{\mathsf{A}}_{U}^{(f)}={\mathsf{P}}_{0,0}-{\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}-(-1)^{f}a^{-1}a_{L_{l^{+}}}{\mathsf{P}}_{f,1}+(-1)^{f}a{\mathsf{P}}_{f+1,1}\,.

(79)

with the proviso that, an additional rescaling of
{\mathsf{P}}_{i,j}
by appropriate powers of the specialized framing variable
a_{\Lambda_{U}}=a_{L}
should be taken into account, according to (
63
).

The skein valued partition function annihilated by this operator is then
related to the generating series of HOMFLYPT polynomials considered in
[
7
]
by a change of framing (
60
), namely

{\mathsf{Z}}^{(f)}_{U}={\mathsf{Z}}^{(f)}_{U;\lambda}=\sum_{\lambda}((-1)^{|\lambda|}q^{\kappa(\lambda)})^{f}\left[\prod_{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.31883pt\vbox{\kern 0.00002pt\kern 0.15941pt\hbox{\kern 0.31883pt\vbox to5.31883pt{\vss\hbox to5.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-5.31883pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt}\kern-0.15941pt\kern-5.31883pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt}\cr}}\kern 345.4pt}\in\lambda}\frac{aq^{c(\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.31883pt\vbox{\kern 0.00002pt\kern 0.15941pt\hbox{\kern 0.31883pt\vbox to5.31883pt{\vss\hbox to5.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-5.31883pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt}\kern-0.15941pt\kern-5.31883pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt}\cr}}\kern 345.4pt})}-a^{-1}q^{-c(\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.31883pt\vbox{\kern 0.00002pt\kern 0.15941pt\hbox{\kern 0.31883pt\vbox to5.31883pt{\vss\hbox to5.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-5.31883pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt}\kern-0.15941pt\kern-5.31883pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt}\cr}}\kern 345.4pt})}}{q^{h(\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.31883pt\vbox{\kern 0.00002pt\kern 0.15941pt\hbox{\kern 0.31883pt\vbox to5.31883pt{\vss\hbox to5.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-5.31883pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt}\kern-0.15941pt\kern-5.31883pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt}\cr}}\kern 345.4pt})}-q^{-h(\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.31883pt\vbox{\kern 0.00002pt\kern 0.15941pt\hbox{\kern 0.31883pt\vbox to5.31883pt{\vss\hbox to5.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-5.31883pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt}\kern-0.15941pt\kern-5.31883pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt}\cr}}\kern 345.4pt})}}\right]{\mathsf{W}}_{\lambda,\emptyset}\,.

(80)

In framing zero this factorizes into a product of two disks, see (
243
) below.

We finally discuss the dependence on the capping path
\eta
. The role of the capping path is simply to write the recursion relation in the skein of the torus
{\rm{Sk}}(T^{2})
, which is natural from the point of view of Chern Simons theory. From the geometric view point of curve counting the recursion relation rather lives in the the skein of the torus minus the two Reeb chord endpoints (one needs to perform an oriented blow up near the endpoints to keep track of framing data). In any case the relative recursion is more refined than the recursion in
{\rm{Sk}}(T^{2})
and gives rise to many recursions there. Geometrically, that corresponds to the choice of capping path
\eta
. As the capping path is a dummy curve that lies above all actual holomorphic curve it does not interfere with the actual dynamics of holomorphic curves and any capping path gives rise to a recursion.

For an example of this we consider the change of capping path shown in Figure
12
.

Figure 12
:
The Legendrian torus of the unknot with a different choice of capping path.

Consider first the effect of capping path change on the original framing zero recursion. We get the new recursion (see below for details)

{\mathsf{A}}_{U;{l^{+}}}^{[0,1]}=a_{L_{l^{+}}}{\mathsf{P}}_{0,1}-{\mathsf{P}}_{1,1}-a_{L_{l^{+}}}a^{-1}\frac{1}{2}((q+q^{-1}){\mathsf{P}}_{0,2}+(q-q^{-1})({\mathsf{P}}_{0,1})^{2})+a{\mathsf{P}}_{1,2}\,,

(81)

which then as explained above annihilates the original zero-framing partition function
Z

{\mathsf{A}}_{U;{l^{+}}}^{[0,1]}\cdot{\mathsf{Z}}_{U;{l^{+}}}=0.

(82)

We point out that the operator
{\mathsf{A}}_{U}^{[0,1]}
is not related to
{\mathsf{A}}_{U}
in any algebraically simple way.
To see the geometric origin of the operator in (
81
), we consider how curves at infinity change between Figures
11
and
12
.
Observe that, in addition to shifting homology classes of curve boundaries by the same amount
(0,1)
, there is an overall rescaling of
{\mathsf{P}}_{0,0}
(which becomes
{\mathsf{P}}_{0,1}
) by a power of
a_{\Lambda_{U}}
.
The reason for this is evident from Figure
12
where the concatenation of
\eta^{\prime}\star\gamma_{00}
has a self-intersection.
On the other hand, while a similar self-intersection is also present for the concatenation of
\eta^{\prime}\star\gamma_{01}
, in this case no rescaling by
a_{L_{{l^{+}}}}
is present.
This is because the combination of terms in homology class
(0,2)
from (
81
) corresponds to a connected twice-around curve, which does not have any such a self-crossing (recall (
51
)).

6.3.2
L_{m^{\pm}}
– the complement filling

In the complement filling
L_{m^{\pm}}
it is the
(0,1)
curve that bounds a disk in the Lagrangian, while the
(1,0)
curve at infinity plays the role of the longitude. To take this into account we would have to re-define how the algebra of
P_{i,j}
operators acts on the
{\mathsf{W}}_{\lambda,\bar{\mu}}
basis of this new skein module. In the new definition we would have that
{\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}
would add a box and
{\mathsf{P}}_{0,1}
would now act diagonally.

We take instead an alternative route, and change basis on the homology
H_{1}(\Lambda)
of the torus at infinity by the
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
transformation
S^{-1}
whose action is defined as

{\mathsf{P}}_{i,j}\to a^{j}\,{\mathsf{P}}_{-j,i}\,.

(83)

Here we also implemented a change of 4-chains, which is accounted for by the area factor
a^{j}
, see Section
7.3.3
for an explanation of the geometric origin of this shift.
With this change of labeling, the recursion for the conormal filling (
77
) becomes

{\mathsf{A}}_{U;\mu}={\mathsf{P}}_{0,0}-a_{L_{m^{+}}}^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}_{0,1}-{\mathsf{P}}_{-1,0}+a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}_{-1,1}

(84)

The solution to the recursion relation

{\mathsf{A}}_{U;{m^{+}}}\cdot{\mathsf{Z}}_{U,{m^{+}}}=0

(85)

is the complement filling skein-valued partition function

{\mathsf{Z}}^{c}_{U,{m^{+}}}={\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\mathrm{di}}^{(1)}\,\cdot\,{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\mathrm{di}}^{(0)}[a^{2}]=\sum_{\lambda}\prod_{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.31883pt\vbox{\kern 0.00002pt\kern 0.15941pt\hbox{\kern 0.31883pt\vbox to5.31883pt{\vss\hbox to5.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-5.31883pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt}\kern-0.15941pt\kern-5.31883pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt}\cr}}\kern 345.4pt}\in\lambda}\frac{q^{c(\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.31883pt\vbox{\kern 0.00002pt\kern 0.15941pt\hbox{\kern 0.31883pt\vbox to5.31883pt{\vss\hbox to5.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-5.31883pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt}\kern-0.15941pt\kern-5.31883pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt}\cr}}\kern 345.4pt})}-a^{2}q^{-c(\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.31883pt\vbox{\kern 0.00002pt\kern 0.15941pt\hbox{\kern 0.31883pt\vbox to5.31883pt{\vss\hbox to5.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-5.31883pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt}\kern-0.15941pt\kern-5.31883pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt}\cr}}\kern 345.4pt})}}{q^{h(\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.31883pt\vbox{\kern 0.00002pt\kern 0.15941pt\hbox{\kern 0.31883pt\vbox to5.31883pt{\vss\hbox to5.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-5.31883pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt}\kern-0.15941pt\kern-5.31883pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt}\cr}}\kern 345.4pt})}-q^{-h(\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.31883pt\vbox{\kern 0.00002pt\kern 0.15941pt\hbox{\kern 0.31883pt\vbox to5.31883pt{\vss\hbox to5.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-5.31883pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt}\kern-0.15941pt\kern-5.31883pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt}\cr}}\kern 345.4pt})}}\,{\mathsf{W}}_{\lambda}\,.

(86)

6.3.3
L_{d}
– the middle leg filling

For the middle leg filling
L_{d}
the contractible curve is
(1,-1)
.
As in the case of the complement filling we change basis for
H_{1}(\Lambda)
to take this into account.
In this case we use
ST
which generates an
{\mathbb{Z}}_{3}
subgroup of
SL(2,{\mathbb{Z}})
, i.e.
(ST)^{3}=1

{\mathsf{P}}_{i,j}\to(-1)^{i}{\mathsf{P}}_{j,-i-j}\,.

(87)

Again we implement an change of the 4-chain. The sign change can be understood as a result of a change in the spin structure.
The skein-valued recursion operator becomes

{\mathsf{A}}_{U;d}={\mathsf{P}}_{0,0}+a_{L_{d}}^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}_{0,-1}-{\mathsf{P}}_{1,-1}-a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}_{1,-2}

(88)

The solution to the recursion relation is

\begin{split}{\mathsf{Z}}_{U;d}&=\sum_{\lambda,\overline{\mu}}\left(\prod_{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.31883pt\vbox{\kern 0.00002pt\kern 0.15941pt\hbox{\kern 0.31883pt\vbox to5.31883pt{\vss\hbox to5.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-5.31883pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\vrule 345.4pt})}}\right)a^{2|\overline{\mu}|}\sum_{\sigma,\overline{\tau},\kappa}c_{\kappa\lambda}^{\sigma}c_{\kappa\mu}^{\tau}W_{\sigma,\overline{\tau}}\end{split}

(89)

where
c_{\kappa\lambda}^{\sigma}
are Littlewood-Richardson coefficients. Note that
{\mathsf{Z}}_{U;d}
is the product of two disc partition functions whose boundaries go along the longitude of
L_{d}
in opposite directions.

6.4
The unlink

We consider two unlinked unknots. Here, holomorphic curves can be read off from big disks and flow lines at infinity as in
[
10
]
. Alternatively, one can shrink the unknot components and move them far apart. After that an action argument shows that the unknot moduli spaces of the degree one Reeb chord of each component cannot contain any curves with negative Reeb chord asymptotics. This means that the worldsheet skein ideal contains both unknot worldsheet skein operator polynomials
{\mathsf{A}}_{U_{1}}
and
{\mathsf{A}}_{U_{2}}
.

Specializing to the skein we then have the generators of the ideal given by

{\mathsf{A}}_{U_{1}}\otimes 1,\qquad 1\otimes{\mathsf{A}}_{U_{2}}.

We have Lagrangian fillings given by combinations of the conormal, complement, and middle leg fillings of the unknot components and the ideal generators imply that in all cases the partition functions are products and there are no contributions from curves stretching between the branes:

{\mathsf{Z}}_{L_{U_{1};s}\cup L_{U_{2};t}}={\mathsf{Z}}_{L_{1;s}}\otimes{\mathsf{Z}}_{L_{2;t}},\quad s,t\in\{l^{\pm},m^{\pm},d\}.

We point out that this contains non-trivial information for the conormal-complement filling. The complement filling
L_{U_{2};m}
say intersects the conormal of
L_{U_{1};l}
in a circle before shifting that after shifting gives a basic holomorphic annulus stretching between
L_{U_{1};l^{+}}
and
L_{U_{2};m^{+}}
. Now stretching first around the complement
L_{U_{2};m^{+}}
and then around
S^{3}
we find four disks appearing from the two basic annuli. If the framing variable for
S^{3}
is
a
and that for the complement of
U_{2}
is
a_{L_{2}}
then these disks have homology classes
a_{L_{2}}a
,
a_{L_{2}}a^{-1}
,
a_{L_{2}}a^{-1}
, and
a_{L_{2}}^{-1}a^{-1}
. Here
a_{L_{2}}a
and one of the
a_{L_{2}}a^{-1}
are anti-disks whereas
a_{L_{2}}^{-1}a^{-1}
and one of the
a_{L_{2}}a^{-1}
are disks and we find that the total contribution of the four disks are that of two disks
a_{L_{2}}a
and
a_{L_{2}}^{-1}a^{-1}
in line with the split ideal provided framing variables are specialized correctly.

6.5
Basic holomorphic annuli

In
[
22
]
an induced skein relation near a basic holomorphic annulus was found.
Here we consider the ambient space
T^{\ast}S^{1}\times\mathbb{C}^{2}
with Lagrangians
S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
S^{1}\times P
, where
P
is a Lagrangian plane close to
\mathbb{R}^{2}
. We then shift the second Lagrangian along a 1-form and find a basic holomorphic annuls. At infinity the Lagrangian has ideal boundary
\Lambda_{1}\cup\Lambda_{2}
, where
\Lambda_{1}\cup\Lambda_{2}
intersects each fiber
S^{3}
in a Legendrian Hopf link. As explained in
[
22
]
, after small perturbation
\Lambda_{1}\cup\Lambda_{2}
has two degree
0
Reeb chords
p
and
q
connecting different components of
\Lambda
, and two degree
1
Reeb chords
c_{1}
and
c_{2}
, starting and ending on the same component and we can find all holomorphic curves. This gives the operator equation in the worldsheet skein
{\rm{Sk}}(T^{\ast}S^{1}\times\mathbb{C}^{2},S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\sqcup S^{1}\times P)

{{\mathsf{P}}}_{0,0}^{\mathrm{ws},(1)}-{{\mathsf{P}}}_{1,0}^{\mathrm{ws},(1)}={{\mathsf{P}}}_{0,0}^{\mathrm{ws},(2)}-{{\mathsf{P}}}_{1,0}^{\mathrm{ws},(2)}\,.

(90)

This then leads to an operator relation in the relative skein of
S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\sqcup S^{1}\times P
,

({{\mathsf{P}}^{\prime}}_{0,0}^{(1)}-{{\mathsf{P}}^{\prime}}_{1,0}^{(1)})\otimes a_{\Lambda_{2}}=a_{\Lambda_{1}}\otimes({{\mathsf{P}}^{\prime}}_{0,0}^{(2)}-{{\mathsf{P}}^{\prime}}_{1,0}^{(2)})\,,

(91)

or choosing capping paths

({\mathsf{P}}_{0,0}^{(1)}-{\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}^{(1)})\otimes a_{\Lambda_{2}}=a_{\Lambda_{1}}\otimes({\mathsf{P}}_{0,0}^{(2)}-{\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}^{(2)}).

(92)

This recursion relation was found combining two moduli spaces of curves with positive puncture at
c_{1}
and
c_{2}
to cancel curves with negative punctures at
p
and
q
. Here we take direct a approach and simply compute the contributions from the canceled terms. The curves at infinity with positive puncture at
c_{1}
are (except for
{\mathsf{P}}_{0.0}
and
{\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}
) a triangle with negative punctures at the mixed chords
p
and
q
. We compute the curves on the filling
L_{1}\cup L_{2}
with the corresponding positive asymptotics.

Proposition 6.1
.

For each integer
m
there is exactly one disk with two positive punctures with boundary corresponding to the curve
C^{\prime}_{m}
in Figure
13
in each Lagrangian and no other holomorphic curves.

Proof.

We use the correspondence between holomorphic curves and Morse flow trees
[
28
]
.
As
P
degenerates to
\mathbb{R}^{2}
and the
1
-form used for the shift goes to zero, holomorphic curves limit to flow trees. The flow trees in question must be a flow line since there are locally only two sheets. This flow line is attached to the boundary of a holomorphic disk with boundary on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
and positive puncture at the limit chord corresponding to
c_{1}
. See Figure
14
. ∎

Figure 13
:
The curve
{\mathsf{C}}^{\prime}_{m}
connecting Reeb chord endpoints.

Figure 14
:
Top: the vector field corresponding to the difference of the two Lagrangians. Middle: the projection of the holomorphic disk with flow line attached to a slice for
m=0
near the limit. Bottom: the boundary of the curve corresponding to a disk with flow line attached.

We denote by
{\mathsf{C}}_{m}
the closed curve in the solid torus in homology class
m
which has exactly
m-1
positive crossings. Proposition
6.1
then leads to the following infinite recursion.

Theorem 6.2
.

The skein operator

{{\mathsf{P}}^{\prime}}_{0,0}^{(1)}-{{\mathsf{P}}^{\prime}}_{1,0}^{(1)}+(q-q^{-1})\sum_{k>0}r\left({\mathsf{C}}^{\prime}_{k},\cdot\right)\otimes{\mathsf{C}}_{k}

(93)

annihilates the annulus skein valued partition function
{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\mathrm{an}}^{(0,0)}
where

{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\mathrm{an}}^{(f_{1},f_{2})}:=\sum_{\lambda}\left[(-1)^{|\lambda|}q^{\kappa(\lambda)}\right]^{f_{1}+f_{2}}\ {\mathsf{W}}^{(1)}_{\lambda,\emptyset}\otimes{\mathsf{W}}^{(2)}_{\lambda,\emptyset}

(94)

This is the only solution with initial condition
0
in all negative classes.

Proof.

The fact that the skein valued partition function is annihilated follows from the usual boundary of moduli space argument once we observe that the disks in Proposition
6.1
lie outside the central annulus. Uniqueness follows by expanding in the
{\mathsf{W}}_{\lambda,\overline{\mu}}
basis which is an eigenbasis for
{\mathsf{P}}_{0,0}-{\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}
.

See also
[
23
]
for a combinatorial proof of this relation.
∎

Remark 6.3
.

We point out the following general application of the worldsheet skein
D
-modules that generalizes well-known uses of augmentation varieties. Consider a Lagrangian
L
with negative end along a Legendrian
\Lambda
. Then we can treat
L
like a closed Lagrangian when it comes to holomorphic disk counting by counting disks with negative asymptotics at Reeb chords of
\Lambda
and then applying the augmentation at the negative ends. Similarly, we can use skein recursions to fill in negative ends of Lagrangians. We use this construction to define a skein module for two
3
-dimensional Lagrangians that intersect cleanly along a circle. In the notation above for unshifted
S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
S^{1}\times P
we define
{\rm{Sk}}(S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\cup S^{1}\times P)
as generated by isotopy classes of curves that are allowed to switch Lagrangian along the intersection and by imposing the relative recursion relation for an annulus at crossings. A more detailed study of this immersed skein module will appear elsewhere.

7
Skein valued recursion for the Hopf link

In this section we derive the skein recursion for the Hopf link from knowledge of holomorphic curves with Reeb chord asymptotics at its ideal contact boundary.

Figure 15
:
The Hopf link with negative crossings.

The main new ingredient compared to earlier examples are the appearance of degree zero Reeb chords. When there are no such chords recursion relations can be read off directly from the moduli spaces of holomorphic curves with asymptotics at degree one Reeb chords. Here such moduli spaces have broken curves in their boundary and the recursion relation appears when the inside pieces of such curves are eliminated algebraically. We describe the curves in Section
7.1
, carry out the elimination theory and prove Theorem
2.1
in Section
7.2
. In Section
7.4
we discuss auxiliary aspects of the recursion relation.

7.1
Holomorphic curves on the Legendrian conormal of the Hopf link

As for any knot or link, holomorphic curves on the Legendrian conormal can be computed in the limit when the conormal approaches a multiple of the unknot conormal where the holomorphic curves on the conormal correspond to holomorphic curves on the unknot conormal with flow trees attached. Here the flow trees are described by the link conormal front over the unknot conormal.

For the Hopf link conormal Legendrian, the Morse flow, the unknot disks and resulting holomorphic curves were determined in
[
2
]
. We use a more symmetric Morse flow here that results from isotoping the Legendrian. The resulting Morse flow is shown in Figure
16
. To see the isotopy, recall that the Hopf link results from a perturbation of a Bott-Morse Legendrian where the Bott manifold of Reeb chords are the stable manifolds of
a_{ij}
. The isotopy that takes us from
[
2
]
to Figure
16
simply slides
a_{21}
and
b_{21}
along its Bott-manifold.

Figure 16
:
Legendrian tori of the Hopf link conormals. The oriented basis
(\lambda_{j},\mu_{j})
are induced by the natural orientation on the complement filling.

As mentioned above, we follow the SFT-terminology of
[
2
]
, and call the curve count at infinity the Hamiltonian. We write
{\mathsf{H}}^{\textnormal{ws}}(c,a_{1},\dots,a_{m})
for the worldsheet skein valued count of holomorphic curves with positive punctures at the degree one chord
c
and degree 0 chords
a_{1},\dots,a_{m}
. We compute relevant parts of the Hamiltonian directly from Figure
16
.

7.1.1
Reeb chords of the Hopf-link Legendrian

The Reeb chords are

c_{11},\;c_{12},\;c_{21},\;c_{22},\;b_{12},\;b_{21}

(99)

of degree
1
and

a_{12},\;a_{21}

(100)

of degree
0
.

7.1.2
Curves with positive puncture at
c_{11}

There are the following five curves with positive puncture at
c_{11}
,

(101)

where the curves
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3}
arise from attaching Morse flow lines to
\text{D}_{00}
as shown in Figure

. Here,

is the disk that has boundary

on

and thus boundary

on

.
The signs in this and similar formulas below are determined by signs of the big disks and intersection signs of (oriented) boundaries of big disks with flow lines attached to them as in

7.2
Proof of Theorem
2.1
– worldsheet skein
D
-module generators

In this section we prove the first part of Theorem
2.1
. We first show that the augmentation variety is generated by augmentations of Lagrangian fillings, then we derive the three recursion relations. We then show in Section
7.3
that these determine skein valued partition functions uniquely.

7.2.1
Augmentations and Lagrangian fillings

The augmentation variety of the Hopf link is cut out by the following three equations in
(\mathbb{C}^{\ast})^{4}
, see
[
1
]
,

\begin{split}A_{1}\ &=\ (1-y_{1}-x_{1}+a^{2}x_{1}y_{1})-(1-y_{2}-x_{2}+a^{2}x_{2}y_{2})\ =\ 0,\\
A_{2}^{\prime}\ &=\ (1-y_{1}-x_{1}+a^{2}x_{1}y_{1})(y_{1}-x_{2})\ =\ 0,\\
A_{3}^{\prime}\ &=\ (1-y_{1}-x_{1}+a^{2}x_{1}y_{1})(x_{1}-y_{2})\ =\ 0.\end{split}

(105)

There are two main branches in the augmentation variety, each of dimension two,

\begin{split}V_{\Gamma}^{(1)}\ &=\ \left\{x_{1}=y_{2},\quad x_{2}=y_{1}\right\},\\
V_{\Gamma}^{(2)}\ &=\ \left\{1-y_{1}-x_{1}+a^{2}x_{1}y_{1}=0,\quad 1-y_{2}-x_{2}+a^{2}x_{2}y_{2}=0\right\}.\end{split}

(106)

The branches intersect along a curve (a ‘diagonal unknot curve’):

V_{\Gamma}^{(1)}\cap V_{\Gamma}^{(2)}\ =\ \left\{x_{1}=y_{2},\;x_{2}=y_{1},\;1-y_{1}-x_{1}+a^{2}x_{1}y_{1}=0\right\}.

(107)

Along
V_{\Gamma}^{(1)}
augmentations are induced by the Lagrangian filling which is the complement of the Hopf link, with the topology
T^{2}\times\mathbb{R}
. Thinking of the Hopf link as two fibers of the Hopf fibration it is easy to see that the complement of the Hopf link is a Lagrangian that admits an exact deformation which is disjoint from the 0-section in
T^{\ast}S^{3}
. Exactness then implies that the Lagrangian does not support any holomorphic curve and the associated partition function is simply
1
and the corresponding
homomorphism

{\mathsf{H}}_{\text{BRST};\Gamma}\to{\rm{Sk}}(T^{2}\times\mathbb{R}),

then simply takes
1
to
1
.

The branch
V_{\Gamma}^{(2)}
is simply the product of the augmentation varieties of the unknot components of the Hopf link,
V^{(2)}=V_{U_{1}}\times V_{U_{2}}
and has augmentations induced by combinations of the fillings of the unknot components.

Noting that the complement of an unknot component is Hamiltonian isotopic to the conormal of the other component we find that along
V_{\Gamma}^{(2)}
we have skein homomorphisms

{\mathsf{H}}_{\Gamma;\text{BRST}}\to{\rm{Sk}}(L_{1}\cup L_{2}),

where
L_{j}\approx S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
and where
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
are either conormal or complement fillings of components according to the asymptotics. Counting curves on these Lagrangian fillings gives wave functions. Below we obtain recursion relations by elimination in the skein and therefore the curve counting wave function satisfies the recursion relation by skein invariance and the description of the boundary of 1-dimensional moduli spaces. The first statement of the theorem follows.

7.2.2
Skein valued elimination theory

We next turn to the world sheet skein
D
-module generators. We use Proposition
4.2
and elimination.

The operator
{\mathsf{A}}^{\textnormal{ws}}_{1}
from
{\mathsf{H}}^{\textnormal{ws}}(c_{11})
and
{\mathsf{H}}^{\textnormal{ws}}(c_{22})

We consider the Hamiltonians of Reeb chords
c_{11},c_{22}
, see (
101
) – (
102
).
In each case there is only one disk with negative punctures, the last one which is a triangle.
Note that both have the
same
negative punctures and that the corresponding terms in the boundary of the 1-dimensional moduli spaces that arise by gluing rigid curves from the inside with corresponding positive Reeb chord asymptotics can then be identified provided that we find capping surfaces for
c_{11}
and
c_{22}
so that the two triangles at infinity can be identified.

The desired identification is obtained by capping
c_{11}
with a path
\gamma_{1}
such that

\alpha_{1}\star\gamma_{1}\star\alpha_{3}=\beta_{2}\,,

(108)

and capping
c_{22}
with a path
\gamma_{2}
such that

\beta_{3}\star\gamma_{2}\star\beta_{1}=\alpha_{2}\,.

(109)

We do not draw the capping paths, but it is easy to see that they are as follows

\begin{split}\gamma_{1}&\simeq\alpha_{1}^{-1}\star\beta_{2}\star\alpha_{3}^{-1}\simeq(\gamma^{(1)}_{00})^{-1}\,,\\
\gamma_{2}&\simeq\beta_{3}^{-1}\star\alpha_{2}\star\beta_{1}^{-1}\simeq(\gamma^{(2)}_{00})^{-1}\,.\\
\end{split}

(110)

If we then choose the capping surfaces

\begin{split}\text{D}_{1}\simeq(\text{D}^{(1)}_{00})^{-1}\,,\\
\text{D}_{2}\simeq(\text{D}^{(2)}_{11})^{-1}\,,\end{split}

(111)

where inversion denotes orientation reversal, we have that

\raisebox{-0.5pt}{
\labellist
\pinlabel{$\text{D}_{00}$} [tr] at 130 110

\pinlabel{$\text{D}_{1}$} [tr] at 125 178

\labellist
\pinlabel{$\text{D}_{11}$} [tr] at 130 110
\pinlabel{$\gamma_{2}$} [tr] at 120 220
\pinlabel{$\text{D}_{2}$} [tr] at 125 178
\endlabellist\includegraphics[scale={.350}]{wsHc22_a21_a12_capped_new}}\ .

(112)

Thus capping
{\mathsf{H}}^{\textnormal{ws}}(c_{ii})
with
\text{D}_{i}
and eliminating triangles by taking the difference of the corresponding operator equations we get from (

) – (

)

\begin{split}{\mathsf{A}}_{1}^{\textnormal{ws}}&={\mathsf{H}}^{\textnormal{ws}}(c_{11};\gamma_{1})-{\mathsf{H}}^{\textnormal{ws}}(c_{22};\gamma_{2})\\
&\simeq\left(\text{D}_{00}^{(1)}\right)^{-1}\cdot\left(\text{D}_{00}^{(1)}-\text{D}_{10}^{(1)}-\text{D}_{01}^{(1)}+\text{D}_{11}^{(1)}\right)-\left(\text{D}_{11}^{(2)}\right)^{-1}\cdot\left(\text{D}_{11}^{(2)}-\text{D}_{01}^{(2)}-\text{D}_{10}^{(2)}+\text{D}_{00}^{(2)}\right)\,.\end{split}

(113)

The operator
{\mathsf{A}}^{\textnormal{ws}}_{2}
from
{\mathsf{H}}^{\textnormal{ws}}(c_{12},a_{21})
,
{\mathsf{H}}^{\textnormal{ws}}(c_{11})
and
{\mathsf{H}}^{\textnormal{ws}}(c_{22})

To obtain a recursion relation from

a_{12}
and

and can be canceled by the corresponding terms in

\labellist

(114)

where

If these capping paths for

\begin{split}\alpha_{1}^{\prime}\star\gamma_{1}^{\prime}&=\alpha_{1}\star\delta_{1}^{\prime}\star\alpha_{3}\\

where

\begin{split}\gamma_{1}^{\prime}&=(\alpha_{1}^{\prime\prime})^{-1}\star\beta_{2},\\

(116)

From this we see that we can choose

with a flow line to

(117)

The operator
{\mathsf{A}}^{\textnormal{ws}}_{3}
from

,

, we obtain a recursion relation from
{\mathsf{H}}^{\textnormal{ws}}(c_{21},a_{12})
by eliminating negative puncture curves.
By symmetry between Morse flows on the two Legendrian tori
\Lambda_{1}

\begin{split}{\mathsf{A}}^{\textnormal{ws}}_{3}&\simeq-\left(\text{D}_{10}^{(2)}\right)^{-1}\cdot\left(\text{D}_{00}^{(2)}-\text{D}_{10}^{(2)}-\text{D}_{01}^{(2)}+\text{D}_{11}^{(2)}\right)\\
&+\left(\text{D}_{10}^{(1)}\right)^{-1}\cdot\left(\text{D}_{11}^{(1)}-\text{D}_{01}^{(1)}-\text{D}_{10}^{(1)}+\text{D}_{00}^{(1)}\right)-\text{A}_{1}^{\prime}-\text{A}_{2}^{\prime}\,,\end{split}

(121)

for two annuli

and

), (

), (

) shows that
{\mathsf{A}}_{i}^{\mathrm{ws}}
,

7.3
Proof of Theorem
2.1
– specialization of
{\mathsf{A}}_{i}^{\mathrm{ws}}
and uniqueness

We describe the homomorphism
\rho_{L}
that maps the (universal) worldsheet skein
D
-module
{\mathsf{D}}^{\mathrm{ws}}_{\Gamma}
to the skein
D
-module
{\mathsf{D}}_{L}={\rm{Sk}}(\partial L)/{\mathsf{I}}_{L}
for Lagrangian fillings over corresponding to points in the augmentation variety
V_{\Gamma}
and prove uniqueness of solutions to the skein recursions.

7.3.1
Lagrangian fillings of the Legendrian Hopf link conormal

Recall that the augmentation variety
V_{\Gamma}
can be considered as the moduli space of (equivalence classes of Floer theoretically unobstructed) Lagrangian fillings of the Legendrian tori
\Lambda_{\Gamma}
at infinity. Recall further that the variety has two branches
V_{\Gamma}=V_{\Gamma}^{(1)}\cup V_{\Gamma}^{(2)}
, see (
106
). The branch
V^{(1)}
is the surface parameterized by the two periods along the exact filling
L_{0}
with topology
T^{2}\times\mathbb{R}
, we treat this case in Section
7.3.6
. The branch
V^{(2)}
is the product of the augmentation curves of the two unknot components of
\Gamma
. We use notation
L_{st}
,
s,t\in\{l^{\pm},m^{\pm},d\}
to denote these fillings. This notation is explained pictorially in Figure
21
, where
L_{st}
refers to a Lagrangian filling with
L_{1}
on the branch labeled by
s
and
L_{2}
labeled by
t
. Note that the roles of
l^{\pm}
and
m^{\pm}
are reverted between the components reflecting the fact that for the Hopf link the meridian of one component is the longitude of the other.

Figure 21
:
Lagrangian fillings of the Hopf link Legendrian over points in
V_{\Gamma}^{(2)}
.

We will prove that the partition function
{\mathsf{Z}}_{L_{st}}
is uniquely determined by
\rho_{L_{st}}({\mathsf{A}}_{i}^{\mathrm{ws}})
for
(s,t)\in\{(l^{+},l^{+}),(l^{+},m^{+}),(l^{+},d),(d,d)\}
. Other cases follow using various symmetries. Consider for example
L_{l^{+},l^{-}}
. Here we shift the conormal
L_{2;l}
in the negative rather than the positive direction. This means that the second of the two basic annuli changes orientation both in the conormal and in
S^{3}
. Thus, the component in
S^{3}
is the positive Hopf link. The same effect can be obtained by starting from
L_{l^{+}l^{+}}
, reverting the orientation of
S^{3}
and changing basis in the conormal of the second component. Uniqueness of
L_{l^{+}l^{+}}
then implies uniqueness for
L_{l^{+}l^{-}}
. Next observing that the complement Lagrangian of one component is Lagrangian isotopic to the conormal Lagrangian of the other we relate
L_{l^{\pm},d}
and
L_{m^{\pm},d}
and relabeling components we get further combinations. When the Lagrangians lie on the same leg we must consider their relative position here again relabeling reduces to one case. By these symmetries it follows that it is sufficient to consider the cases listed above. To simplify notation we will denote them simply
L_{ll}
,
L_{lm}
,
L_{ld}
, and
L_{dd}
.

7.3.2
Two unknot conormals –
L_{ll}

With
{\mathsf{A}}_{i}=\rho_{\Lambda}({\mathsf{A}}_{i}^{\textnormal{ws}})
, the recursion operators at infinity in the case of the conormal-conormal filling read

\displaystyle{\mathsf{A}}_{1}

\displaystyle=\left({\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0}-a^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,0}-a^{-1}a_{\Lambda_{1}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,1}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,1}\right)\otimes a_{\Lambda_{2}}

(122)

\displaystyle\quad-a_{\Lambda_{1}}\otimes\left({\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,0}-a^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{1,0}-a^{-1}a_{\Lambda_{2}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,1}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{1,1}\right)\,

(123)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{A}}_{2}

\displaystyle=a_{\Lambda_{1}}\left(-a{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{-1,0}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0}\right)\otimes 1+\left(a_{\Lambda_{1}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{-1,1}-a{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,1}\right)\otimes(a_{\Lambda_{2}})^{2}

(124)

\displaystyle+1\otimes a_{\Lambda_{2}}\left(a(a_{\Lambda_{2}})^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,-1}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{1,-1}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,0}+a{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{1,0}\right)\,

(125)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{A}}_{3}

\displaystyle=1\otimes a_{\Lambda_{2}}\left(-a{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{-1,0}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,0}\right)+(a_{\Lambda_{1}})^{2}\otimes\left(a_{\Lambda_{2}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{-1,1}-a{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,1}\right)

(126)

\displaystyle+a_{\Lambda_{1}}\left(a(a_{\Lambda_{1}})^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,-1}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,-1}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0}+a{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,0}\right)\otimes 1\,.

(127)

Here,
\rho_{\Lambda}
is defined via the choices of framing vector fields and 4-chains described below. The correct powers of
a
and
a_{\Lambda_{i}}
can be determined by counting intersections of the present holomorphic curves with the 4-chains of the Lagrangians. Alternatively, these are the unique powers up to an overall scaling so that the operators (
129
), (
130
), and (
131
) determined below annihilate the HOMFLYPT generating function of the Hopf link.

Denote the two components of the filling
L_{l;i}\approx S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
,
i=1,2
.
We use coordinates on
L_{l;1}
and
L_{l;2}
so that the operators
{\mathsf{P}}^{(i)}_{k,l}
on
\Lambda_{i}
act on the skein of
L_{l;i}
by the formulas described in Section
5.2
, and we substitute
a_{\Lambda_{i}}\to a_{L_{i}}
, where
a_{L_{i}}
denotes the
a
-variable corresponding to
L_{l;i}
. We choose the framing vector field along the central
S^{1}\subseteq L_{l;i}
and the 4-chains
C_{i}
of
L_{l;i}
in such a way that the central
S^{1}
has vanishing self-linking in these coordinates and that the
C_{i}
do not intersect any of the present Lagrangians. The framing vector field of
S^{3}
is chosen so that the two components of the Hopf link, framed by this vector field, have self-linking number
+1
. As a consequence, the 4-chain
C_{S^{3}}
will intersect
L_{l;i}
in a
(-1,1)
-curve (note that the orientation on
L_{l;i}
we use is opposite to the orientation induced by
S^{3}
). Therefore, when acting with the operators
{\mathsf{P}}^{(i)}_{k,l}
on the partition function with boundary near the central
S^{1}
, we need to rescale the operators according to

{\mathsf{P}}^{(i)}_{k,l}\mapsto a^{k+l}{\mathsf{P}}^{(i)}_{k,l}

(128)

to obtain the correct action. The recursion relation thus reads:

\displaystyle{\mathsf{A}}_{1}=\

\displaystyle=\ \left({\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,0}-a_{L_{1}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,1}+a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,1}\right)\otimes a_{L_{2}}

(129)

\displaystyle\quad-a_{L_{1}}\otimes\left({\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{1,0}-a_{L_{2}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,1}+a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{1,1}\right),

\displaystyle{\mathsf{A}}_{2}\

\displaystyle=\ a_{L_{1}}\left(-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{-1,0}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0}\right)\otimes 1+\left(a_{L_{1}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{-1,1}-a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,1}\right)\otimes(a_{L_{2}})^{2}

(130)

\displaystyle\quad+1\otimes a_{L_{2}}\left((a_{L_{2}})^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,-1}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{1,-1}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,0}+a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{1,0}\right),

\displaystyle{\mathsf{A}}_{3}\

\displaystyle=\ 1\otimes a_{L_{2}}\left(-{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{-1,0}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,0}\right)+(a_{L_{1}})^{2}\otimes\left(a_{L_{2}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{-1,1}-a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,1}\right)

(131)

\displaystyle\quad+a_{L_{1}}\left((a_{L_{1}})^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,-1}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,-1}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0}+a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,0}\right)\otimes 1\,.

We show next that these operators determine a partition function with initial conditions corresponding to the conormal filling uniquely. The partition function takes values only in the non-negative part
\widehat{{\rm{Sk}}}_{+}(L_{l;1})\otimes\widehat{{\rm{Sk}}}_{+}(L_{l;2})
of the skein modules of
L_{l;1}
and
L_{l;2}
(i.e., curves in non-negative homology classes).
Let

,

be two partition functions with degree

and consider

(132)

Assume that
{\mathsf{Z}}_{k}
,

\left(\left({\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}^{(1)}-{\mathsf{P}}_{0,0}^{(1)}\right)\otimes a_{L_{1}}^{-1}-a_{L_{2}}^{-1}\otimes\left({\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}^{(2)}-{\mathsf{P}}_{0,0}^{(2)}\right)\right){\mathsf{Z}}_{k}=\sum_{i,j\geq 0,(i,j)\neq(0,0)}A_{i,j}({\mathsf{Z}}_{k})

(133)

\left(\left(a_{L_{1}}{\mathsf{P}}_{1,-1}^{(1)}-{\mathsf{P}}_{0,-1}^{(1)}\right)\otimes 1\right){\mathsf{Z}}_{k}=\sum_{i,j\geq 0}B_{i,j}({\mathsf{Z}}_{k}).

7.3.3
One unknot conormal and one unknot complement –
L_{lm}

As the curves relevant to the skein recursion lie at infinity they are unaffected by the particular choice of filling. However, the
4
-chains do depend on the filling and we need to trace how they affect the recursion.

We choose the 4-chain
C_{2}^{\prime}
of
L_{2;m}
by gluing the 4-chains
C_{S^{3}}
of
S^{3}
and the previous 4-chain
C_{2}
of
L_{2;l}
. We modify
C_{2}
so that it intersects
S^{3}
in a parallel (unlinked) copy of the second component of the Hopf link. The intersection curve then has linking number
-1
with the first Hopf link component and the effect on the partition function is the substitution

{\mathsf{W}}^{(1)}_{\lambda,\overline{\mu}}\mapsto a_{L_{2}}^{-|\lambda|+|\mu|}{\mathsf{W}}^{(1)}_{\lambda,\overline{\mu}},

(136)

which changes the operators in (
122
), (
124
), and (
126
) after the substitution
a_{\Lambda_{i}}\to a_{L_{i}}
according to

{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{i,j}\mapsto a_{L_{2}}^{-j}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{i,j}.

(137)

Here we use the same symbol
a_{L_{2}}
to denote the
a
-variable corresponding to both
L_{2;l}
and
L_{2;m}
. Now we glue
S^{3}
to
L_{2;l}
and the 4-chain
C_{S^{3}}
of
S^{3}
to the 4-chain
C_{2}
of
L_{2;l}
to obtain the complement filling
L_{2;m}
with 4-chain
C_{2}^{\prime}
. Since at infinity,
C_{2}^{\prime}=C_{2}\cup C_{S^{3}}
, we get the induced change of variables
a\mapsto aa_{\Lambda_{2}}
in relation to Section
7.3.2
.

Since the cycle
(0,1)
in
\Lambda_{2}
contracts in
L_{2;m}
and
(1,0)
does not, it is convenient to change basis on
H_{1}(\Lambda_{2})
by

{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{i,j}\mapsto{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{-j,i}\,.

(138)

This relabeling then preserves the orientation of
\Lambda_{2}
and the skein algebra (
49
). In the new basis, the operators at infinity become

\displaystyle{\mathsf{A}}_{1}

\displaystyle=\left({\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0}-a^{-1}a_{\Lambda_{2}}^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,0}-a^{-1}a_{\Lambda_{1}}a_{\Lambda_{2}}^{-2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,1}+a_{\Lambda_{2}}^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,1}\right)\otimes a_{\Lambda_{2}}

(139)

\displaystyle\quad-a_{\Lambda_{1}}\otimes\left({\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,0}-a^{-1}a_{\Lambda_{2}}^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,1}-a^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{-1,0}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{-1,1}\right)\,,

(140)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{A}}_{2}

\displaystyle=a_{\Lambda_{1}}\left(-aa_{\Lambda_{2}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{-1,0}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0}\right)\otimes 1+\left(a_{\Lambda_{1}}a_{\Lambda_{2}}^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{-1,1}-a{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,1}\right)\otimes(a_{\Lambda_{2}})^{2}

(141)

\displaystyle+1\otimes a_{\Lambda_{2}}\left(a{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{1,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{1,1}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,0}+aa_{\Lambda_{2}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,1}\right)\,,

(142)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{A}}_{3}

\displaystyle=1\otimes a_{\Lambda_{2}}\left(-aa_{\Lambda_{2}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,-1}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,0}\right)+(a_{\Lambda_{1}})^{2}\otimes\left(a_{\Lambda_{2}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{-1,-1}-aa_{\Lambda_{2}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{-1,0}\right)

(143)

\displaystyle+a_{\Lambda_{1}}\left(a(a_{\Lambda_{1}})^{-1}(a_{\Lambda_{2}})^{2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,-1}-a_{\Lambda_{2}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,-1}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0}+aa_{\Lambda_{2}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,0}\right)\otimes 1\,.

(144)

Finally, we choose the framing vector field of
S^{3}
along the intersection
S^{3}\cap L_{2;m}
before shifting
L_{2;m}
so that this circle has self-linking number
+1
, and we choose framing vector field of
L_{2;m}
and perform the shifts of
L_{1}
and
L_{2;m}
in such a way that near the intersection of
L_{1}\cap L_{2;m}
, the framing vector field of
L_{2;m}
induces trivial self-linking for this intersection and
L_{1}
is shifted relative to
L_{2;m}
in the direction of a 1-form dual to the tangent vector of the first component of the Hopf link. This induces the rescaling
{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{i,j}\mapsto a^{-i+j}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{i,j}
since
C_{S^{3}}
now intersects
L_{2;m}
in a
(-1,-1)
-curve, and
{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{i,j}\mapsto a^{i+j}a_{L_{2}}^{i}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{i,j}
since
C_{2;m}
intersects
L_{1}
in a
(0,1)
-curve and
C_{S^{3}}
intersects
L_{1}
in a
(-1,1)
-curve. We then get the final form of the operators

\displaystyle{\mathsf{A}}_{1}\

\displaystyle=\ \left({\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,0}-a_{L_{1}}a_{L_{2}}^{-2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,1}+a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,1}\right)\otimes a_{L_{2}}

(145)

\displaystyle\quad-a_{L_{1}}\otimes\left({\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,0}-a_{L_{2}}^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,1}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{-1,0}+a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{-1,1}\right),

\displaystyle{\mathsf{A}}_{2}\

\displaystyle=\ a_{L_{1}}\left(-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{-1,0}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0}\right)\otimes 1+\left(a_{L_{1}}a_{L_{2}}^{-2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{-1,1}-a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,1}\right)\otimes(a_{L_{2}})^{2}

(146)

\displaystyle\quad+1\otimes a_{L_{2}}\left({\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{1,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{1,1}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,0}+a^{2}a_{L_{2}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,1}\right),

\displaystyle{\mathsf{A}}_{3}\

\displaystyle=\ 1\otimes a_{L_{2}}\left(-a_{L_{2}}^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,-1}+a_{L_{2}}^{-2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,0}\right)+(a_{L_{1}})^{2}\otimes\left(a_{L_{2}}^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{-1,-1}-a^{2}a_{L_{2}}^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{-1,0}\right)

(147)

\displaystyle\quad+a_{L_{1}}\left((a_{L_{1}})^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,-1}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,-1}-a_{L_{2}}^{-2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0}+a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,0}\right)\otimes 1\,,

(where
{\mathsf{A}}_{3}
was multiplied by
a_{L_{2}}^{-2}
) that are the image of
{\mathsf{A}}_{i}^{\mathrm{ws}}
under
\rho_{L_{lm}}
that generate the HOMFLYPT skein

-module for
\partial L_{lm}
.

The skein valued recursion operators (

), (
146
), and (

) uniquely determine the skein valued partition function of

. With coordinates as above, the partition function is supported in degrees
(i,j)
such that

and

, and its degree

. We again use induction on
(i,j)
. Assuming that the degree

to (

), (

) is uniquely determined by the conditions on

for all

is uniquely determined by (

\sum_{\lambda\vdash i}b_{\lambda}{\mathsf{W}}_{\lambda,\emptyset}\otimes{\mathsf{W}}_{\emptyset,\overline{\lambda}}.

(148)

Here the coefficients

are uniquely determined by (

).

7.3.4
A middle leg Lagrangian and an unknot conormal –
L_{dl}

We obtain the recursion in this case from the case of
L_{ll}
by applying the coordinate change

{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{i,j}\mapsto(-1)^{j}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{j,-i-j}

(149)

(moving the first brane to the middle leg) and adjusting signs and powers of
a_{L_{1}}
with the result

\displaystyle{\mathsf{A}}_{1}\

\displaystyle=\ \left({\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0}-(a_{L_{1}})^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,-1}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,-1}-a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,-2}\right)\otimes a_{L_{2}}

(150)

\displaystyle\quad-a_{L_{1}}\otimes\left({\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{1,0}-(a_{L_{1}})^{-2}a_{L_{2}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,1}+a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{1,1}\right),

\displaystyle{\mathsf{A}}_{2}\

\displaystyle=\ \left(-a_{L_{1}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,1}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0}-(a_{L_{2}})^{2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,0}+a^{2}(a_{L_{2}})^{2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,-1}\right)\otimes 1

(151)

\displaystyle\quad+a_{L_{1}}\otimes\left({\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,-1}-a_{L_{2}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{1,-1}-(a_{L_{1}})^{-2}a_{L_{2}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,0}+a^{2}a_{L_{2}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{1,0}\right),

\displaystyle{\mathsf{A}}_{3}\

\displaystyle=\ a_{L_{2}}\otimes\left(-{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{-1,0}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,0}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{-1,1}-a^{2}(a_{L_{1}})^{2}(a_{L_{2}})^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,1}\right)

(152)

\displaystyle\quad+a_{L_{1}}\left(-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{-1,1}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{-1,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0}+a^{2}a_{L_{1}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,-1}\right)\otimes 1\,.

Here the support of the partition function involves also the
a
-grading. If
t
denotes the
a
-degree and
(i,j)
are as usual then the partition function is supported in degrees
(i,j,t)
, where
j\geq 0
,
i+2t\geq 0
, and
