. Uniqueness follows as before.

7.3.5
Two middle leg conormals –
L_{dd}

In this case we also move the second Lagrangian in
L_{dl}
to the middle leg and accordingly change coordinates on
\Lambda_{2}
according to
{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{i,j}\mapsto(-1)^{i}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{i,-i-j}
. The recursion becomes

\displaystyle{\mathsf{A}}_{1}\

\displaystyle=\ \left({\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0}-(a_{L_{1}})^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,-1}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,-1}-a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,-2}\right)\otimes a_{L_{2}}

(153)

\displaystyle\quad-a_{L_{1}}\otimes\left({\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,0}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,-1}-(a_{L_{1}})^{-2}a_{L_{2}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{1,-1}-a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{1,-2}\right),

\displaystyle{\mathsf{A}}_{2}\

\displaystyle=\ \left(-a_{L_{1}}(a_{L_{2}})^{-2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,1}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,0}+a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,-1}\right)\otimes a_{L_{2}}

(154)

\displaystyle\quad+a_{L_{1}}\otimes\left((a_{L_{2}})^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{-1,1}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{-1,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,0}-a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,-1}\right),

\displaystyle{\mathsf{A}}_{3}\

\displaystyle=\ a_{L_{2}}\otimes\left({\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,1}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{1,0}-a^{2}(a_{L_{1}})^{2}(a_{L_{2}})^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{1,-1}\right)

(155)

\displaystyle\quad+a_{L_{1}}\left(-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{-1,1}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{-1,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0}+a^{2}a_{L_{1}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,-1}\right)\otimes 1\,.

Here, we rescaled
{\mathsf{A}}_{2}
by
(a_{L_{2}})^{-1}
and used that
(a_{L_{2}})^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0}-(a_{L_{1}})^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,0}=a_{L_{2}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0}-a_{L_{1}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,0}
.

To bring the operators into a more symmetric form, we change the framing on the second brane:
{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{i,j}\mapsto{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{i+j,j}
. The resulting operators read

\displaystyle{\mathsf{A}}_{1}\

\displaystyle=\ \left({\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0}-(a_{L_{1}})^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,-1}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,-1}-a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,-2}\right)\otimes a_{L_{2}}

(156)

\displaystyle\quad-a_{L_{1}}\otimes\left({\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,0}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{-1,-1}-(a_{L_{1}})^{-2}a_{L_{2}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,-1}-a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{-1,-2}\right),

\displaystyle{\mathsf{A}}_{2}\

\displaystyle=\ \left(-a_{L_{1}}\left(a_{L_{2}}\right)^{-2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,1}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,0}+a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,-1}\right)\otimes a_{L_{2}}

(157)

\displaystyle\quad+a_{L_{1}}\otimes\left(\left(a_{L_{2}}\right)^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,1}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{-1,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,0}-a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{-1,-1}\right),

\displaystyle{\mathsf{A}}_{3}\

\displaystyle=\ a_{L_{2}}\otimes\left({\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{1,1}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{1,0}-a^{2}(a_{L_{1}})^{2}(a_{L_{2}})^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,-1}\right)

(158)

\displaystyle\quad+a_{L_{1}}\left(-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{-1,1}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{-1,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0}+a^{2}a_{L_{1}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,-1}\right)\otimes 1\,.

The partition function is supported on
(i,j,t)
with
i+2t\geq 0
,
i+2t+j\geq 0
, and
t\geq 0
. Uniqueness follows as for
L_{ll}
.

7.3.6
The Hopf link complement –
L_{0}

Lastly, we consider the Hopf link complement filling
L_{0}\approx T^{2}\times\mathbb{R}
. To obtain the operators at infinity, we proceed as for
L_{lm}
, but instead of gluing
C_{1}
to
C_{S^{3}}
, we glue
C_{1}
to
C^{\prime}_{2}
to obtain the 4-chain
C
. At infinity, the 4-chain becomes the union
C=C_{1}\cup C^{\prime}_{2}
, and we set
a_{\Lambda_{1}}=a_{\Lambda_{2}}=a_{\Lambda}
.

We then choose the coordinates on
L_{0}\cong T^{2}\times\mathbb{R}
in such a way that
{\mathsf{P}}_{i,j}^{(2)}
now acts as multiplication by
{\mathsf{P}}_{i,j}
from above in the skein of
T^{2}
and
{\mathsf{P}}_{i,j}^{(1)}
acts as multiplication by
{\mathsf{P}}_{-i,j}
from below. The operators acting on the skein of
L
become

\displaystyle{\mathsf{A}}_{1}\

\displaystyle=\ a^{-1}[{\mathsf{P}}_{0,1},\,\cdot\,]+a^{-1}[{\mathsf{P}}_{-1,0},\,\cdot\,]-[{\mathsf{P}}_{-1,1},\,\cdot\,]\,,

(159)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{A}}_{2}\

\displaystyle=\ a[{\mathsf{P}}_{1,0},\,\cdot\,]-[{\mathsf{P}}_{1,1},\,\cdot\,]+a[{\mathsf{P}}_{0,1},\,\cdot\,]\,,

(160)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{A}}_{3}\

\displaystyle=\ -a[{\mathsf{P}}_{0,-1},\,\cdot\,]+[{\mathsf{P}}_{-1,-1},\,\cdot\,]-a[{\mathsf{P}}_{-1,0},\,\cdot\,]\,,

(161)

where in addition to the described changes we rescaled
{\mathsf{A}}_{2}
and
{\mathsf{A}}_{3}
by a factor of
a_{L}^{-2}
.

Given the initial condition that the degree
(0,0)
-part of the partition function
{\mathsf{Z}}
is equal to
1
and
{\mathsf{Z}}
is non-vanishing only in non-negative degrees, uniqueness follows again by induction on the degree
(i,j)
: assuming that we have already proven uniqueness in degrees
(k,l)\neq(i,j)
with
k\leq i
and
l\leq j
, then the degree
(i-1,j)
-part of
{\mathsf{A}}_{1}({\mathsf{Z}})
implies uniqueness in degree
(i,j)
if
j>0
since
\left[{\mathsf{P}}_{-1,0},{\mathsf{P}}_{i,j}\right]=(q^{-j}-q^{j}){\mathsf{P}}_{i-1,j}
. If
j=0
and
i\neq 0
, then the degree
(i-1,-1)
-part of
{\mathsf{A}}_{3}({\mathsf{Z}})
proves the desired uniqueness since
\left[{\mathsf{P}}_{-1,-1},{\mathsf{P}}_{i,0}\right]=(q^{i}-q^{-i}){\mathsf{P}}_{i-1,-1}
.

This completes the proof of Theorem
2.1
.∎

7.4
Framing and reduction to quantum curves

In this section we discuss tow further aspects of Theorem
2.1
.

7.4.1
Skein recursion identities in generic framing

As before we obtain skein recursions in arbitrary framings by straightforward change of variables (
62
) applied to
{\mathsf{A}}_{j}
,
j=1,2,3
for the filling
L_{ll}
(with partition function given by all colored HOMFLYPT)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathsf{A}}_{1}^{(f_{1},f_{2})}&=\left({\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,0}-(-1)^{f_{1}}a_{\Lambda_{1}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{f_{1},1}+(-1)^{f_{1}}a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1+f_{1},1}\right)\otimes a_{\Lambda_{2}}\\
&-a_{\Lambda_{1}}\otimes\left({\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{1,0}-(-1)^{f_{2}}a_{\Lambda_{2}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{f_{2},1}+(-1)^{f_{2}}a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{1+f_{2},1}\right),\end{split}

(162)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathsf{A}}_{2}^{(f_{1},f_{2})}&=a_{\Lambda_{1}}\left(-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{-1,0}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0}\right)\otimes 1+(-1)^{f_{1}}\left(a_{\Lambda_{1}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{-1+f_{1},1}-a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{f_{1},1}\right)\otimes(a_{\Lambda_{2}})^{2}\\
&+1\otimes a_{\Lambda_{2}}\left((-1)^{f_{2}}(a_{\Lambda_{2}})^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{-f_{2},-1}-(-1)^{f_{2}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{1-f_{2},-1}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,0}+a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{1,0}\right)\,,\end{split}

(163)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathsf{A}}_{3}^{(f_{1},f_{2})}&=1\otimes a_{\Lambda_{2}}\left(-{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{-1,0}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,0}\right)+(a_{\Lambda_{1}})^{2}\otimes(-1)^{f_{2}}\left(a_{\Lambda_{2}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{-1+f_{2},1}-a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{f_{2},1}\right)\\
&+a_{\Lambda_{1}}\left((-1)^{f_{1}}(a_{\Lambda_{1}})^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{-f_{1},-1}-(-1)^{f_{1}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1-f_{1},-1}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0}+a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,0}\right)\otimes 1\,.\end{split}

(164)

These operators define the skein
D
-module of the (conormal-conormal filling of the) framed Hopf link.

7.4.2
Specialization to the
U(1)
skein

The
U(1)
limit on each of the branes
L_{1},L_{2}
is defined by

a_{\Lambda_{i}}\to q\qquad{\mathsf{P}}^{(k)}_{\pm 1,0}\to\hat{x}_{k}^{\pm 1}\qquad{\mathsf{P}}^{(k)}_{0,\pm 1}\to\hat{y}_{k}^{\pm 1}

with
\hat{y}_{k}\hat{x}_{r}=q^{2\delta_{kr}}\hat{y}_{k}\hat{x}_{r}
.
From the above relations it follows also that

{\mathsf{P}}^{(k)}_{1,1}\to-q\hat{x}_{k}\hat{y}_{k}\qquad{\mathsf{P}}^{(k)}_{-1,1}\to-q^{-1}\hat{x}_{k}\hat{y}_{k}^{-1}\qquad{\mathsf{P}}^{(k)}_{1,-1}\to q^{-1}\hat{x}_{k}^{-1}\hat{y}_{k}\,.

Using these in the skein valued recursion operators (
113
), (
119
) and (
121
) gives

\displaystyle\hat{A}_{1}

\displaystyle=q\left[\left(1-\hat{y}_{1}-q\hat{x}_{1}+a^{2}q\hat{x}_{1}\hat{y}_{1}\right)-\left(1-\hat{y}_{2}-q\hat{x}_{2}+a^{2}q\hat{x}_{2}\hat{y}_{2}\right)\right],

\displaystyle\hat{A}_{2}

\displaystyle=q\left(-\hat{y}_{1}^{-1}+1+q\hat{x}_{1}\hat{y}_{1}^{-1}-q\,a^{2}\,\hat{x}_{1}\right)+q\left(q^{-1}\hat{x}_{2}^{-1}-q^{-1}\hat{x}_{2}^{-1}\hat{y}_{2}-1+a^{2}\,\hat{y}_{2}\right),

\displaystyle\hat{A}_{3}

\displaystyle=q\left(-\hat{y}_{2}^{-1}+1+q\hat{x}_{2}\hat{y}_{2}^{-1}-q\,a^{2}\,\hat{x}_{2}\right)+q\left(q^{-1}\hat{x}_{1}^{-1}-q^{-1}\hat{x}_{1}^{-1}\hat{y}_{1}-1+a^{2}\,\hat{y}_{1}\right)

In agreement with the quantum
U(1)
-relations in
[
1
]

Taking the semi-classical limit of the three skein recursions (
), we get the augmentation variety of the Hopf link cut out by the three equations (
105
).
Recall that these equations define an augmentation variety with two branches (
106
) which intersect along the one dimensional locus (
107
).
Viewing the augmentation variety as the characteristic variety of the
D
-module determined by the quantized versions
\hat{A}_{j}
,
j=1,2,3
, the phenomenon that branches of the augmentation variety intersect in codimension one is related to the irreducibility of the
D
-module.

8
Generalized quiver structures of skein valued curve counts

The skein valued recursion operators determined above determines the partition functions of the Lagrangian fillings, for the conormal the HOMFLYPT polynomials colored by arbitrary partitions. In this section we describe these, and how they can be viewed as being generated by basic disks and annuli with linking boundaries, generalizing the quiver description of holomorphic curves for knots and links proposed in
[
10
,
21
]
.

8.1
Basic curves

As a preliminary step towards a skein valued quiver description, we discuss the building blocks. We first discuss the building blocks themselves and how they interact through a natural algebraic structure in the skein.

8.1.1
The disk

The skein valued disk partition function in framing
f
is, see Section
6.2
:

{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\mathrm{di}}^{(f)}:=\sum_{\lambda}((-1)^{|\lambda|}q^{\kappa(\lambda)})^{f}\left[\prod_{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.31883pt\vbox{\kern 0.00002pt\kern 0.15941pt\hbox{\kern 0.31883pt\vbox to5.31883pt{\vss\hbox to5.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-5.31883pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt}\kern-0.15941pt\kern-5.31883pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt}\cr}}\kern 345.4pt}\in\lambda}\frac{-q^{-c(\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.31883pt\vbox{\kern 0.00002pt\kern 0.15941pt\hbox{\kern 0.31883pt\vbox to5.31883pt{\vss\hbox to5.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-5.31883pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt}\kern-0.15941pt\kern-5.31883pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt}\cr}}\kern 345.4pt})}}{q^{h(\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.31883pt\vbox{\kern 0.00002pt\kern 0.15941pt\hbox{\kern 0.31883pt\vbox to5.31883pt{\vss\hbox to5.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-5.31883pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt}\kern-0.15941pt\kern-5.31883pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt}\cr}}\kern 345.4pt})}-q^{-h(\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.31883pt\vbox{\kern 0.00002pt\kern 0.15941pt\hbox{\kern 0.31883pt\vbox to5.31883pt{\vss\hbox to5.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-5.31883pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt}\kern-0.15941pt\kern-5.31883pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt}\cr}}\kern 345.4pt})}}\right]{\mathsf{W}}_{\lambda,\emptyset}\ \in\ {\rm{Sk}}(S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}),

which coincides with the partition function of a toric brane in
{\mathbb{C}}^{3}
, and as such it satisfies the skein recursion obtained from (
72
) by specializing
a_{\Lambda}=a_{L}
and by applying the framing transformations (
62
) and (
63
)

({\mathsf{P}}_{0,0}-{\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}+(-1)^{1-f}a_{L}{\mathsf{P}}_{f,1})\;{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\mathrm{di}}^{(f)}\ =\ 0,

(171)

where the extra 4-chain intersection with
L
gives the effective rescaling
{\mathsf{P}}_{f,1}\mapsto a_{L}^{-f}{\mathsf{P}}_{f,1}
.

8.1.2
The annulus

The skein valued annulus partition function in framing
(f_{1},f_{2})
is, see Section
6.5
:

{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\mathrm{an}}^{(f_{1},f_{2})}:=\sum_{\lambda}\left[(-1)^{|\lambda|}q^{\kappa(\lambda)}\right]^{f_{1}+f_{2}}\ {\mathsf{W}}_{\lambda,\emptyset}\otimes{\mathsf{W}}_{\lambda,\emptyset}\ \in\ {\rm{Sk}}(S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})\otimes{\rm{Sk}}(S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}).

(172)

It satisfies the following identity

\left(a_{L_{1}}^{-1}({\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,0})\otimes 1-1\otimes a_{L_{2}}^{-1}({\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{1,0})\right)\,{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\mathrm{an}}^{(f_{1},f_{2})}\ =\ 0,

(173)

that follows from the (infinite) recursion

\left(a_{L_{1}}^{-1}({\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0})-(q-q^{-1})\sum_{n\geq 1}{{\mathsf{C}}_{n}}^{(1;f_{1})}\otimes{{\mathsf{C}}_{n}}^{(2;f_{2})}\right)\,{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\mathrm{an}}^{(f_{1},f_{2})}\ =\ 0

(174)

where
{\mathsf{C}}_{n}^{(a;f)}
is a curve on brane
L_{a}
winding
n
times around the longitude with
n-1
positive self-crossings framing changed to framing
f
.

8.1.3
The twisted annulus

An alternative annulus is obtained by taking one of the boundaries to wind along the anti-meridian of the two solid tori:

{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\widetilde{\mathrm{an}}}^{(f_{1},f_{2})}:=\sum_{\lambda}\left[(-1)^{|\lambda|}q^{\kappa(\lambda)}\right]^{f_{1}+f_{2}}\ {\mathsf{W}}_{\lambda,\emptyset}\otimes{\mathsf{W}}_{\emptyset,\overline{\lambda}}\ \in\ \widehat{{\rm{Sk}}}(S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})\otimes\widehat{{\rm{Sk}}}\textasciiacute(S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})

(175)

which in framing
(0,0)
is annihilated by the following operators:

\displaystyle{\mathsf{B}}_{1}\

\displaystyle=\ \left({\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,0}\right)\otimes a_{L_{2}}-a_{L_{1}}\otimes\left({\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{-1,0}\right)\,,

(176)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{B}}_{2}\

\displaystyle=\ a_{L_{1}}\left(-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{-1,0}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0}\right)\otimes 1+1\otimes a_{L_{2}}\left({\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{1,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,0}\right)\,,

(177)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{B}}_{3}\

\displaystyle=\ \left((a_{L_{1}})^{-i}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{i,1}-(a_{L_{2}})^{-2i}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,1}\right)\otimes 1-1\otimes\left((a_{L_{2}})^{-i}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{-i,1}-(a_{L_{2}})^{-2i}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,1}\right)\,,

(178)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{B}}_{4}\

\displaystyle=\ \left((a_{L_{1}})^{-i}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{i,-1}-(a_{L_{1}})^{-2i}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,-1}\right)\otimes 1-1\otimes\left((a_{L_{2}})^{-i}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{-i,-1}-(a_{L_{1}})^{-2i}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,-1}\right)

(179)

which follow directly from the action of
{\mathsf{P}}_{i,j}
on
{\mathsf{W}}_{\lambda,\overline{\mu}}
described in Section
5.2
.

8.1.4
The anti-annulus

The skein valued anti-annulus partition function is:

{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\overline{\mathrm{an}}}^{(f_{1},f_{2})}=\sum_{\lambda}\left[(-1)^{|\lambda|}q^{\kappa(\lambda)}\right]^{f_{1}-f_{2}}{\mathsf{W}}_{\lambda,\emptyset}\otimes{\mathsf{W}}_{\lambda^{t},\emptyset}

(180)

where we used
\kappa(\lambda)=-\kappa(\lambda^{t})
, which satisfies the following identity

\left(a_{L_{1}}^{-1}({\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,0})\otimes 1-1\otimes a_{L_{2}}({\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{-1,0})\right)\;{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\overline{\mathrm{an}}}^{(f_{1},f_{2})}\ =\ 0,

(181)

that follows from the (infinite) recursion

\left(a_{L_{1}}^{-1}({\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0})-(q-q^{-1})\sum_{n\geq 1}{{\mathsf{C}}_{n}}^{(1;f_{1})}\otimes{{\mathsf{C}}_{n}^{\ast}}^{(2;f_{2})}\right)\,{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\overline{\mathrm{an}}}^{(f_{1},f_{2})}\ =\ 0

(182)

where
{{\mathsf{C}}_{n}^{\ast}}^{f}
is a curve winding
n
times around the longitude of a brane with
n-1
negative self-crossings framing changed to framing
f
.

We will use the basic curve partition functions in Sections
8.1.1
–
8.1.4
with additional homology variables. We write
{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\mathrm{di}}^{(f)}[\xi]
for the partition function from
{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\mathrm{di}}^{(f)}
by replacing
{\mathsf{W}}_{\lambda}
by
\xi^{|\lambda|}{\mathsf{W}}_{\lambda}
. We use similar notation for the annuli partition functions.

8.2
The algebra structure on
{\rm{Sk}}(S^{1}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2})

We will also use the algebra structure of the HOMFLYPT skein module
{\rm{Sk}}(L)
. We recall its definition and discuss an extension describing products of linked curves.

The product of (unlinked) elements of
{\rm{Sk}}(L)
is obtained by viewing the solid torus as the product
A\times I
, where
A
is an annulus and
I
an interval.
We multiply one skein element with another by gluing the outer boundary component of one annulus to the inner boundary component of the other. The product is obviously commutative.

In the basis
{\mathsf{W}}_{\lambda,\overline{\mu}}
the algebra is that of universal characters of
GL(N)
defined in
[
43
]

{\mathsf{W}}_{\lambda,\overline{\mu}}\,{\mathsf{W}}_{\nu,\overline{\rho}}=\sum_{\sigma,\overline{\tau}}M^{\sigma,\overline{\tau}}_{\lambda,\overline{\mu};\nu,\overline{\rho}}{\mathsf{W}}_{\sigma,\overline{\tau}}

(183)

where the structure constants
M^{\sigma,\overline{\tau}}_{\lambda,\overline{\mu};\nu,\overline{\rho}}
have an explicit expression in terms of Littlewood-Richardson coefficients
c_{\lambda\mu}^{\nu}
as follows

M^{\sigma,\overline{\tau}}_{\lambda,\overline{\mu};\nu,\overline{\rho}}=\sum_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta}\left[\sum_{\kappa}c_{\kappa\alpha}^{\lambda}c_{\kappa\beta}^{\rho}\right]\left[\sum_{\epsilon}c_{\epsilon\gamma}^{\mu}c_{\epsilon\delta}^{\nu}\right]c^{\sigma}_{\alpha\delta}c^{\tau}_{\beta\gamma}\,.

(184)

Specializing to the
{\mathsf{W}}_{\lambda}:={\mathsf{W}}_{\lambda,\emptyset}
gives in fact the
Littlewood-Richardson product
[
38
]

{\mathsf{W}}_{\lambda}{\mathsf{W}}_{\mu}=\sum_{\nu}c_{\lambda\mu}^{\nu}{\mathsf{W}}_{\nu}\,.

(185)

This result is natural from the physical point of view, as mentioned in Section
2.2
,
{\mathsf{W}}_{\lambda}={\mathsf{W}}_{\lambda,\emptyset}
can be viewed as line operators
in (complexified)
U(N)_{\kappa}
Chern-Simons theory on
S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
and, as such, give a generalization of the Verlinde basis
[
18
,
19
]
.
There is a natural algebraic structure on this basis, corresponding to the fusion algebra, which in the limits of large rank and large level becomes the Littlewood-Richardson product.

Another specialization that will be useful below corresponds to the product of
{\mathsf{W}}_{\lambda,\emptyset}
and
{\mathsf{W}}_{\emptyset,\overline{\rho}}
. In this case (
183
) simplifies to

{\mathsf{W}}_{\lambda,\emptyset}\,{\mathsf{W}}_{\emptyset,\overline{\rho}}=\sum_{\sigma,\overline{\tau}}\left[\sum_{\kappa}c_{\kappa\sigma}^{\lambda}c_{\kappa\tau}^{\rho}\right]{\mathsf{W}}_{\sigma,\overline{\tau}}^{(0,0)}\,.

(186)

Finally we introduce a twisted product on
{\mathsf{W}}_{\lambda}:={\mathsf{W}}_{\lambda,\emptyset}
controlled by a discrete parameter
f\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, and defined as follows

{{\mathsf{W}}_{\lambda}\star_{f}{\mathsf{W}}_{\mu}=\sum_{\nu}c_{\lambda\mu}^{\nu}\,q^{f(\kappa(\nu)-\kappa(\lambda)-\kappa(\mu))}\,{\mathsf{W}}_{\nu}}.

(187)

The case
\star_{0}
is just the usual product, and we omit that in notation.
The product (
183
) correspond to the product in the skein of the annulus
{\rm{Sk}}(S^{1}\times[0,1])
.
The twisted product (
187
) correspond to its framed counterpart (specialized to positive-winding curves).

8.3
Proof of Theorem
2.2

We find solutions to the recursion relations for the various Lagrangian fillings.

8.3.1
The filling
L_{lm}

We find the solution to the recursion relations in Section
7.3.3
.
Recall the unknot partition function (

) corresponding to the product of unlinked disks (in framing zero):

The partition function for the complement of the unknot is obtained by replacing

{\mathsf{Z}}^{c}_{U}={\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\mathrm{di}}^{(1)}\,\cdot\,{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\mathrm{di}}^{(0)}[a^{2}]=\sum_{\lambda}\prod_{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 8.3.2
The filling

The recursion in Section

has the solution

{\mathsf{Z}}=\left(\left({\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\text{di}}\overline{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}^{(0)}_{\text{di}}[a^{2}]\right)\otimes\left({\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\text{di}}{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(1)}_{\text{di}}[a^{2}]\right)\right){\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0,0)}_{\text{an}},

where

\overline{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}^{(0)}_{\text{di}}=\sum_{\lambda}\prod_{\square\in\lambda}\frac{q^{-c(\square)}}{q^{h(\square)}-q^{-h(\square)}}{\mathsf{W}}_{\emptyset,\overline{\lambda}}.

(218)

This is seen as follows. Using the relative recursion relations for the disks, it follows that for any partition function
B
, we have that

\begin{split}\left({\mathsf{P}}_{1,-1}-a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}_{1,-2}\right)\left(B\star{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\text{di}}\star\overline{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}^{(0)}_{\text{di}}[a^{2}]\right)&=\left({\mathsf{P}}_{1,-1}-{\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}\right)(B)\star{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\text{di}}\star\overline{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}^{(0)}_{\text{di}}[a^{2}]\,,\\
\left({\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}-a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}_{1,-1}\right)\left(B\star{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\text{di}}\star\overline{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}^{(0)}_{\text{di}}[a^{2}]\right)&=\left({\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}-{\mathsf{P}}_{1,1}\right)(B)\star{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\text{di}}\star\overline{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}^{(0)}_{\text{di}}[a^{2}]\,,\\
\left({\mathsf{P}}_{-1,0}-{\mathsf{P}}_{-1,1}\right)\left(B\star{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\text{di}}\star\overline{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}^{(0)}_{\text{di}}[a^{2}]\right)&=\left({\mathsf{P}}_{-1,0}-a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}_{-1,-1}\right)(B)\star{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\text{di}}\star\overline{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}^{(0)}_{\text{di}}[a^{2}]\,,\\
\left({\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}-a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}_{1,1}\right)\left(B\star{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\text{di}}\star{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(1)}_{\text{di}}[a^{2}]\right)&=\left({\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}-{\mathsf{P}}_{1,1}\right)(B)\star{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\text{di}}\star{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(1)}_{\text{di}}[a^{2}]\,,\\
\left({\mathsf{P}}_{1,-1}-a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}\right)\left(B\star{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\text{di}}\star{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(1)}_{\text{di}}[a^{2}]\right)&=\left({\mathsf{P}}_{1,-1}-{\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}\right)(B)\star{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\text{di}}\star{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(1)}_{\text{di}}[a^{2}]\,,\\
\left({\mathsf{P}}_{-1,0}-{\mathsf{P}}_{-1,1}\right)\left(B\star{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\text{di}}\star{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(1)}_{\text{di}}[a^{2}]\right)&=\left({\mathsf{P}}_{-1,0}-a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}_{-1,1}\right)(B)\star{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\text{di}}\star{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(1)}_{\text{di}}[a^{2}]\,.\\
\end{split}

(219)

This implies that

is annihilated by

and

if and only if

\displaystyle=\ \left({\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0}-(a_{L_{1}})^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,-1}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,-1}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,0}\right)\otimes a_{L_{2}}

(220)

\displaystyle\quad-a_{L_{1}}\otimes\left({\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{1,0}-(a_{L_{1}})^{-2}a_{L_{2}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,1}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{1,1}\right),

\displaystyle=\ \left(-a_{L_{1}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,1}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0}-(a_{L_{2}})^{2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,0}+(a_{L_{2}})^{2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,1}\right)\otimes 1

(221)

\displaystyle\quad+a_{L_{1}}\otimes\left({\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,-1}-a_{L_{2}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{1,-1}-(a_{L_{1}})^{-2}a_{L_{2}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,0}+a_{L_{2}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{1,0}\right),

\displaystyle=\ a_{L_{2}}\otimes\left(-{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{-1,0}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,0}+a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{-1,1}-a^{2}(a_{L_{1}})^{2}(a_{L_{2}})^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,1}\right)

\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}^{(0,0)}_{\text{an}}=\sum_{\lambda}{\mathsf{W}}_{\lambda,\emptyset}\otimes{\mathsf{W}}_{\emptyset,\overline{\lambda}}

(224)

This is seen as follows: using the relative recursion relations for the disks, it follows that for any partition function
B
, we have that

\begin{split}\left({\mathsf{P}}_{1,-1}-a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}_{1,-2}\right)\left(B\star{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\text{di}}\star\overline{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}^{(0)}_{\text{di}}[a^{2}]\right)&=\left({\mathsf{P}}_{1,-1}-{\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}\right)(B)\star{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\text{di}}\star\overline{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}^{(0)}_{\text{di}}[a^{2}]\,,\\
\left({\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}-a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}_{1,-1}\right)\left(B\star{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\text{di}}\star\overline{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}^{(0)}_{\text{di}}[a^{2}]\right)&=\left({\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}-{\mathsf{P}}_{1,1}\right)(B)\star{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\text{di}}\star\overline{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}^{(0)}_{\text{di}}[a^{2}]\,,\\
\left({\mathsf{P}}_{-1,0}-{\mathsf{P}}_{-1,1}\right)\left(B\star{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\text{di}}\star\overline{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}^{(0)}_{\text{di}}[a^{2}]\right)&=\left({\mathsf{P}}_{-1,0}-a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}_{-1,-1}\right)(B)\star{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\text{di}}\star\overline{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}^{(0)}_{\text{di}}[a^{2}]\,,\\
\left({\mathsf{P}}_{-1,-1}-a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}_{-1,-2}\right)\left(B\star{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(1)}_{\text{di}}\star\overline{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}^{(1)}_{\text{di}}[a^{2}]\right)&=\left({\mathsf{P}}_{-1,-1}-{\mathsf{P}}_{-1,0}\right)(B)\star{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(1)}_{\text{di}}\star\overline{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}^{(1)}_{\text{di}}[a^{2}]\,,\\
\left({\mathsf{P}}_{-1,0}-a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}_{-1,-1}\right)\left(B\star{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(1)}_{\text{di}}\star\overline{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}^{(1)}_{\text{di}}[a^{2}]\right)&=\left({\mathsf{P}}_{-1,0}-{\mathsf{P}}_{-1,1}\right)(B)\star{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(1)}_{\text{di}}\star\overline{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}^{(1)}_{\text{di}}[a^{2}]\,,\\
\left({\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}-{\mathsf{P}}_{1,1}\right)\left(B\star{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(1)}_{\text{di}}\star\overline{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}^{(1)}_{\text{di}}[a^{2}]\right)&=\left({\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}-a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}_{1,-1}\right)(B)\star{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(1)}_{\text{di}}\star\overline{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}^{(1)}_{\text{di}}[a^{2}]\,.\end{split}

(225)

This implies that
{\mathsf{Z}}
is annihilated by
{\mathsf{A}}_{1},{\mathsf{A}}_{2},
and
{\mathsf{A}}_{3}
if and only if
\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}^{(0,0)}_{\text{an}}
is annihilated by the operators

\displaystyle{\mathsf{A}}^{\prime}_{1}\

\displaystyle=\ \left({\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0}-(a_{L_{1}})^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,-1}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,-1}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,0}\right)\otimes a_{L_{2}}

(226)

\displaystyle\quad-a_{L_{1}}\otimes\left({\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,0}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{-1,-1}-\left(a_{L_{1}}\right)^{-2}a_{L_{2}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,-1}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{-1,0}\right),

\displaystyle{\mathsf{A}}^{\prime}_{2}\

\displaystyle=\ \left(-a_{L_{1}}\left(a_{L_{2}}\right)^{-2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,1}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,0}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,1}\right)\otimes a_{L_{2}}

(227)

\displaystyle\quad+a_{L_{1}}\otimes\left(\left(a_{L_{2}}\right)^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,1}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{-1,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{-1,1}\right),

\displaystyle{\mathsf{A}}^{\prime}_{3}\

\displaystyle=\ a_{L_{2}}\otimes\left(a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{1,-1}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{1,0}-a^{2}(a_{L_{1}})^{2}(a_{L_{2}})^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,-1}\right)

(228)

\displaystyle\quad+a_{L_{1}}\left(-a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{-1,-1}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{-1,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0}+a^{2}a_{L_{1}}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,-1}\right)\otimes 1\,.

As before, the corresponding relations are be verified by a straightforward computation.

8.3.4
The filling
L_{0}

To finish the proof we consider the link complement filling, then the operators (
159
), (
160
), and (
161
) in the recursion relation can be written purely as linear combinations of commutators
\left[{\mathsf{P}}_{i,j},\cdot\right]
. Hence,
{\mathsf{Z}}=1
is a solution. This is a manifestation of the fact that
L
can by made disjoint from the zero-section via an exact Lagrangian isotopy.

The results in Sections
8.3.1
,
8.3.2
,
8.3.3
, and
8.3.4
establish the desired result. ∎

8.4
A closed form expression for the Hopf link in all colors

In this section we show that the partition function of all colored HOMFLYPT polynomials of the Hopf link, i.e., the partition function of
L_{ll}
(or
L_{mm}
) can be expressed as the product of four basic disks and the partition function from Section
6.6
that we conjecture to correspond to a basic holomorphic three holed sphere. More precisely, we have the following.

Theorem 8.1
.

The HOMFLYPT partition function of the conormal filling
L_{ll}
(and that of
L_{mm}
) of the Hopf link can be expressed as

{\mathsf{Z}}_{L_{ll}}\ =\ \left(\left({\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\textnormal{di}}\otimes{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\textnormal{di}}\right)\star_{-1}\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}_{(0,3)}^{(-1,-1)}[a]\right)\star_{0}\left({\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\textnormal{di}}^{(1)}[a^{2}]\otimes{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\textnormal{di}}^{(1)}[a^{2}]\right).

(229)

Proof.

Denote the right hand side of (
229
) by
{\mathsf{Z}}
. We show that
{\mathsf{Z}}
satisfies the recursion relations for the Hopf link. The uniqueness part of Theorem
2.1
then gives the result. More precisely we claim that
{\mathsf{Z}}
is annihilated by
{\mathsf{A}}_{1}
and
{\mathsf{A}}_{3}
:

\displaystyle\left(\left(a_{L_{1}}^{-1}\left({\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}^{(1)}-{\mathsf{P}}_{0,0}^{(1)}\right)-a^{2}a_{L_{1}}^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}_{1,1}^{(1)}+{\mathsf{P}}_{0,1}^{(1)}\right)\otimes 1\right){\mathsf{Z}}

(230)

\displaystyle\qquad=\left(1\otimes\left(a_{L_{2}}^{-1}\left({\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}^{(2)}-{\mathsf{P}}_{0,0}^{(2)}\right)-a^{2}a_{L_{2}}^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}_{1,1}^{(2)}+{\mathsf{P}}_{0,1}^{(2)}\right)\right){\mathsf{Z}},

and

\displaystyle\left(\left(a_{L_{1}}{\mathsf{P}}_{1,-1}^{(1)}-{\mathsf{P}}_{0,-1}^{(1)}+a_{L_{1}}\left({\mathsf{P}}_{0,0}^{(1)}-a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}^{(1)}\right)\right)\otimes 1\right){\mathsf{Z}}

(231)

\displaystyle\qquad=\left(1\otimes a_{L_{2}}\left({\mathsf{P}}_{0,0}^{(2)}-{\mathsf{P}}_{-1,0}^{(2)}\right)+a_{L_{1}}^{2}\otimes a_{L_{2}}\left({\mathsf{P}}_{-1,1}^{(2)}-a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}_{0,1}^{(2)}\right)\right){\mathsf{Z}}.

The relation from
{\mathsf{A}}_{2}
then follows by symmetry.

From the relative recursion relation

\left({\mathsf{P}}^{\prime}_{0,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{\prime}_{1,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{\prime}_{1,1}\right)\left({\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\textnormal{di}}^{(1)}\right)=0

(232)

which is a framed
1
1

1

In addition to changing
{\mathsf{P}}^{\prime}_{0,1}\to{\mathsf{P}}^{\prime}_{1,1}
we have also rescaled
{\mathsf{P}}^{\prime}_{1,1}\to a^{-1}_{L}{\mathsf{P}}^{\prime}_{1,1}
to obtain the correct action on the framed solid torus, see Section
5.2
.
version of (
71
),
we have that

{\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}\left(A\star_{0}{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\textnormal{di}}^{(1)}[a^{2}]\right)={\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}\left(A\right)\star_{0}{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\textnormal{di}}^{(1)}[a^{2}]+a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}_{1,1}\left(A\star_{0}{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\textnormal{di}}^{(1)}[a^{2}]\right)

(233)

for any element
A\in\widehat{\textnormal{Sk}}(L)
in the completed skein of the solid torus
L
.

Similarly, the relative recursion relation

\left({\mathsf{P}}^{\prime}_{0,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{\prime}_{1,0}-a_{L}{\mathsf{P}}^{\prime}_{0,1}\right)\left({\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\textnormal{di}}^{(0)}\right)=0

(234)

implies that

{\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}\left(A\star_{-1}{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\textnormal{di}}\right)={\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}(A)\star_{-1}{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\textnormal{di}}-a_{L}{\mathsf{P}}_{0,1}\left(A\star_{-1}{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\textnormal{di}}\right).

(235)

We find that

\displaystyle\left(\left(a_{L_{1}}^{-1}\left({\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0}\right)-a^{2}a_{L_{1}}^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,1}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,1}\right)\otimes 1\right){\mathsf{Z}}\ \overset{\eqref{eq:P_{1,0} on cdot Psi^(0,1)(a)},\eqref{eq:P_{1,0} on fcdot{-1} Psi^-(0,1)(a^{-1})}}{=}

\displaystyle\quad\left(\left({\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\textnormal{di}}\otimes{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\textnormal{di}}\right)\star_{-1}\left(\left({\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{1,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0,0}\right)\otimes a_{L_{1}}^{-1}\right)\left(\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}_{(0,3)}^{(-1,-1)}[a]\right)\right)\star_{0}\left({\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\textnormal{di}}^{(1)}[a^{2}]\otimes{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\textnormal{di}}^{(1)}[a^{2}]\right)\ \overset{\eqref{eq:relation for Z_{(0,3)}),first identity}}{=}

\displaystyle\quad\left(\left({\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\textnormal{di}}\otimes{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\textnormal{di}}\right)\star_{-1}\left(a_{L_{2}}^{-1}\otimes\left({\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{1,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,0}\right)\right)\left(\widetilde{{\mathsf{\Psi}}}_{(0,3)}^{(-1,-1)}[a]\right)\right)\star_{0}\left({\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\textnormal{di}}^{(1)}[a^{2}]\otimes{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\textnormal{di}}^{(1)}[a^{2}]\right)\ \overset{\eqref{eq:P_{1,0} on cdot Psi^(0,1)(a)},\eqref{eq:P_{1,0} on fcdot{-1} Psi^-(0,1)(a^{-1})}}{=}

\displaystyle\quad\left(1\otimes\left(a_{L_{2}}^{-1}\left({\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{1,0}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,0}\right)-a^{2}a_{L_{2}}^{-1}{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{1,1}+{\mathsf{P}}^{(2)}_{0,1}\right)\right){\mathsf{Z}},

and (
230
) follows.

The relative recursion relation (
232
) implies the identities

{\mathsf{P}}_{-1,1}\left(A\star_{0}{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\textnormal{di}}^{(1)}[a^{2}]\right)={\mathsf{P}}_{-1,1}\left(A\right)\star_{0}{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\textnormal{di}}^{(1)}[a^{2}]-a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}_{-1,2}\left(A\star_{0}{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\textnormal{di}}^{(1)}[a^{2}]\right),

(236)

{\mathsf{P}}_{-1,0}\left(A\star_{0}{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\textnormal{di}}^{(1)}[a^{2}]\right)={\mathsf{P}}_{-1,0}\left(A\right)\star_{0}{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\textnormal{di}}^{(1)}[a^{2}]-a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}_{-1,1}\left(A\right)\star_{0}{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\textnormal{di}}^{(1)}[a^{2}],

(237)

and

{\mathsf{P}}_{1,-1}\left(A\star_{0}{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\textnormal{di}}^{(1)}[a^{2}]\right)={\mathsf{P}}_{1,-1}\left(A\right)\star_{0}{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\textnormal{di}}^{(1)}[a^{2}]+a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}\left(A\star_{0}{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\textnormal{di}}^{(1)}[a^{2}]\right)

(238)

for any
A\in\widehat{\textnormal{Sk}}(L)
. Similarly, the relation (
234
) implies the identities

{\mathsf{P}}_{0,1}\left(A\star_{-1}{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\textnormal{di}}\right)={\mathsf{P}}_{0,1}(A)\star_{-1}{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\textnormal{di}}-a_{L}{\mathsf{P}}_{-1,2}\left(A\star_{-1}{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\textnormal{di}}\right),

(239)

{\mathsf{P}}_{-1,0}\left(A\star_{-1}{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\textnormal{di}}\right)={\mathsf{P}}_{-1,0}\left(A\right)\star_{-1}{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\textnormal{di}}+a_{L}{\mathsf{P}}_{-2,1}\left(A\right)\star_{-1}{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\textnormal{di}},

(240)

and

{\mathsf{P}}_{0,-1}\left(A\star_{-1}{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\textnormal{di}}\right)={\mathsf{P}}_{0,-1}\left(A\right)\star_{-1}{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\textnormal{di}}+a_{L}{\mathsf{P}}_{-1,0}\left(A\right)\star_{-1}{\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\textnormal{di}}.

(241)

Furthermore, we observe that for
A=B\star_{-1}C
,

{\mathsf{P}}_{-1,1}(A)={\mathsf{P}}_{-1,1}(B)\star_{-1}C\quad\text{ and }\quad{\mathsf{P}}_{1,-1}(A)={\mathsf{P}}_{1,-1}(B)\star_{-1}C,

(242)

where in the right hand sides
{\mathsf{P}}_{\pm 1,\mp 1}(B)
should be understood as inserting
{\mathsf{P}}_{\pm 1,\mp 1}
on the boundary of a small tubular neighborhood of
B
. Combining (
236
), (
237
), (
239
), (
240
), and (
242
) we obtain

\displaystyle\left(a_{L_{2}}\left({\mathsf{P}}_{0,0}^{(2)}-{\mathsf{P}}_{-1,0}^{(2)}\right)+a_{L_{1}}^{2}\left(a_{L_{2}}{\mathsf{P}}_{-1,1}^{(2)}-a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}_{0,1}^{(2)}\right)\right)\left(\left({\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\textnormal{di}}\star_{-1}A\right)\star_{0}\left({\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\textnormal{di}}^{(1)}[a^{2}]\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\Bigl({\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\textnormal{di}}\ \star_{-1}

\displaystyle\qquad\left(a_{L_{2}}\left({\mathsf{P}}_{0,0}^{(2)}-{\mathsf{P}}_{-1,0}^{(2)}\right)+a_{L_{2}}\left(a^{2}+a_{L_{1}}^{2}\right){\mathsf{P}}_{-1,1}^{(2)}-a_{L_{2}}^{2}{\mathsf{P}}_{-2,1}^{(2)}-a^{2}a_{L_{1}}^{2}{\mathsf{P}}_{0,1}^{(2)}\right)(A)\Bigr)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\star_{0}\left({\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\textnormal{di}}^{(1)}[a^{2}]\right).

Then combining (
238
), (
241
), and (
242
) we obtain

\displaystyle\left(a_{L_{1}}{\mathsf{P}}_{1,-1}^{(1)}-{\mathsf{P}}_{0,-1}^{(1)}+a_{L_{1}}\left({\mathsf{P}}_{0,0}^{(1)}-a^{2}{\mathsf{P}}_{1,0}^{(1)}\right)\right)\left(\left({\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\textnormal{di}}\star_{-1}A\right)\star_{0}\left({\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\textnormal{di}}^{(1)}[a^{2}]\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\Bigl({\mathsf{\Psi}}^{(0)}_{\textnormal{di}}\ \star_{-1}

\displaystyle\qquad\left(a_{L_{1}}{\mathsf{P}}_{1,-1}^{(1)}-{\mathsf{P}}_{0,-1}^{(1)}+a_{L_{1}}\left({\mathsf{P}}_{0,0}^{(1)}-{\mathsf{P}}_{-1,0}^{(1)}\right)\right)(A)\Bigr)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\star_{0}\left({\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\textnormal{di}}^{(1)}[a^{2}]\right).

Thus, relation (
231
) is equivalent to relation (
) in Proposition
. The theorem follows.
∎

8.5
A conjectured skein valued quiver for the Hopf link in all colors

We propose a quiver-like partition function that we conjecture is equivalent to the skein valued partition function for the filling
L_{ll}
(and then also
L_{mm}
). The partition function is constructed from the unknot basic disks on each knot conormal and an annulus and an anti-annulus with boundaries linking in the conormal branes, see Figure
2
, as follows.

Recall the unknot skein valued partition function in zero framing
[
7
]
, see Section
6.3
.
We observe that (

) in framing zero factorizes is a product of two unlinked disks

(243)

\lambda

and anti-annulus

in colors

and

345.4pt}\in\lambda}\frac{a^{2}q^{c(\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.31883pt\vbox{\kern 0.00002pt\kern 0.15941pt\hbox{\kern 0.31883pt\vbox to5.31883pt{\vss\hbox to5.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-5.31883pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt}\kern-0.15941pt\kern-5.31883pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt}\cr}}\kern 345.4pt})}-q^{-c(\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.31883pt\vbox{\kern 0.00002pt\kern 0.15941pt\hbox{\kern 0.31883pt\vbox to5.31883pt{\vss\hbox to5.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-5.31883pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt}\kern-0.15941pt\kern-5.31883pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt}\cr}}\kern 345.4pt})}}{q^{h(\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.31883pt\vbox{\kern 0.00002pt\kern 0.15941pt\hbox{\kern 0.31883pt\vbox to5.31883pt{\vss\hbox to5.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-5.31883pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt}\kern-0.15941pt\kern-5.31883pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt}\cr}}\kern 345.4pt})}-q^{-h(\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.31883pt\vbox{\kern 0.00002pt\kern 0.15941pt\hbox{\kern 0.31883pt\vbox to5.31883pt{\vss\hbox to5.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-5.31883pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\vrule height=5.31883pt,width=0.31883pt}\kern-0.15941pt\kern-5.31883pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt\kern 5.0pt\hrule width=5.63766pt,height=0.31883pt}\cr}}\kern 345.4pt})}}\\
H^{-}_{\mu}&=(-a^{2})^{|\mu|}\\
H_{\nu}^{+}&=q^{-2\kappa(\nu)}.\\
\end{split}

(245)

These are the coefficients of the following partition functions

\begin{split}\sum_{\lambda}H^{O}_{\lambda}\,{\mathsf{W}}_{\lambda}&={\mathsf{Z}}^{(0)}_{U}[a]={\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\mathrm{di}}^{(0)}\,\cdot\,{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\mathrm{di}}^{(1)}[a^{2}]\\
\sum_{\mu}H^{-}_{\mu}\,{\mathsf{W}}_{\mu}\otimes{\mathsf{W}}_{\mu^{t}}&={\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\overline{\mathrm{an}}}^{(0,0)}[a^{2}]\\
\sum_{\nu}H_{\nu}^{+}\,{\mathsf{W}}_{\nu}\otimes{\mathsf{W}}_{\nu}&={\mathsf{\Psi}}_{{\mathrm{an}}}^{(-1,-1)}\end{split}

(246)

Our conjectural form of the Hopf link partition function starts from the unknot disks on each brane:

\begin{split}{\mathsf{Z}}_{\text{disks}}&=\sum_{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}\left(H^{O}_{\lambda_{1}}{\mathsf{W}}_{\lambda_{1}}\otimes 1\right)\star_{0}\left(H^{O}_{\lambda_{2}}1\otimes{\mathsf{W}}_{\lambda_{2}}\right),\\
&={\mathsf{Z}}^{(0)}_{U_{1}}[a]\otimes{\mathsf{Z}}^{(0)}_{U_{2}}[a],\\
&=\left({\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\mathrm{di}}^{(0)}\,\cdot\,{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\mathrm{di}}^{(1)}[a^{2}]\right)\otimes\left({\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\mathrm{di}}^{(0)}\,\cdot\,{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\mathrm{di}}^{(1)}[a^{2}]\right).\end{split}

(247)

Next to these we place an unlinked anti-annulus

\begin{split}{\mathsf{Z}}_{\text{disks+a.a.}}&={\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\overline{\mathrm{an}}}^{(0,0)}[a^{2}]\star_{0}{\mathsf{Z}}_{\text{disks}}\\
&={\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\overline{\mathrm{an}}}^{(0,0)}[a^{2}]\star_{0}\left[\left({\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\mathrm{di}}^{(0)}\,\cdot\,{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\mathrm{di}}^{(1)}[a^{2}]\right)\otimes\left({\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\mathrm{di}}^{(0)}\,\cdot\,{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\mathrm{di}}^{(1)}[a^{2}]\right)\right]\end{split}

(248)

Finally add a framed annulus that links all disks and the anti-annulus with
-1
units of linking on each brane, and obtain the conjectured skein valued partition function of the Hopf Link conormal filling:

\begin{split}{\mathsf{Z}}_{ll}&={\mathsf{\Psi}}_{{\mathrm{an}}}^{(-1,-1)}\star_{-1}{\mathsf{Z}}_{\text{disks+a.a.}}\\
&={\mathsf{\Psi}}_{{\mathrm{an}}}^{(-1,-1)}\star_{-1}\left[{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\overline{\mathrm{an}}}^{(0,0)}[a^{2}]\star_{0}\left[\left({\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\mathrm{di}}^{(0)}\,\cdot\,{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\mathrm{di}}^{(1)}[a^{2}]\right)\otimes\left({\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\mathrm{di}}^{(0)}\,\cdot\,{\mathsf{\Psi}}_{\mathrm{di}}^{(1)}[a^{2}]\right)\right]\right]\end{split}

(249)

Developing the product, we arrive at the following formula for the Hopf link HOMFLYPT polynomials, in terms of contributions from unknot basic disks and the annulus and the anti-annulus

{H^{\text{quiver}}_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}=\sum_{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\mu,\nu}\sum_{\rho_{1},\rho_{2}}q^{-(\kappa^{\sigma_{1}}_{\rho_{1}\nu})}q^{-(\kappa^{\sigma_{2}}_{\rho_{2}\nu})}H^{O}_{\lambda_{1}}H^{O}_{\lambda_{2}}H^{-}_{\mu}H_{\nu}^{+}c_{\lambda_{1}\mu}^{\rho_{1}}c_{\lambda_{2}\mu^{t}}^{\rho_{2}}c_{\rho_{1}\nu}^{\sigma_{1}}c_{\rho_{2}\nu}^{\sigma_{2}}}\,,

(250)

where

\kappa^{\sigma}_{\rho\nu}=\kappa(\sigma)-\kappa(\rho)-\kappa(\nu).

We have checked numerically that these agree with the known expression for HOMFLYPT polynomials, obtained e.g. from the topological vertex, see (
251
).

8.5.1
Remarks on the skein-valued knots-quivers correspondence

The quiver description that we found involves a finite number of basic holomorphic curves, that we denote ‘skein-basic’ curves. In the specialization to the
U(1)
skein, these become the basic holomorphic curves of the standard quiver descriptions of HOMFLYPT polynomials of knots and links (generalized to include annuli), see
[
9
,
10
]
.
As observed in
[
21
]
, basic holomorphic curves in the
U(1)
skein of a knot conormal filling correspond to LMOV invariants (which count open M2 branes) of disks winding once around the longitude of
L
, and all other LMOV invariants are encoded by their linking data.

The skein-basic curves appearing in (
249
) generalize this notion, implying in particular that the LMOV invariants of basic curves ‘stabilize’ when the number of branes on each component of the Hopf link conormal is taken to infinity.
Unlike the basic curves appearing in the
U(1)
quiver description, skein-basic curves encode the HOMFLYPT polynomials colored by arbitrary partitions.
Correspondingly, they also encode information about LMOV invariants colored by arbitrary partitions.

8.6
Moduli space bifurcations and the Hopf link partition function

In this section we outline a proof of Conjecture
2.3
. We first illustrate the argument to derive the description (
243
) of the uknot partition function.

8.6.1
The unknot partition function from bifurcations

Recall the proof of the Ooguri-Vafa conjecture from
[
3
,
22
]
, see Section
3.2
: shift the conormal of the knot off of the zero section. For small shifts there is only the basic annulus stretching between the conormal and the zero section, after sufficient SFT stretching all boundary components shrink and curves leave the zero section and can be transferred to the conifold.

Applying this to the unknot, it is possible to arrange the complex structure so that the boundary of the initial annulus shrinks to a point, without other skein moves. At the time when the boundary component becomes a point there must be already a corresponding moduli space of disks that crosses the Lagrangian at the shrinking moment. Furthermore, right before this moment the whole partition function is carried by the annulus, which means there is also another disk that cancels the contribution of the crossing disk before the crossing.

Now, the disk that does not cross the Lagrangian is the only curve without
a
-powers and hence accounts for the extremal HOMFLYPT of the unknot. We know the corresponding recursion relation and conclude this disk and its multiple covers contribute one of the factors in (
243
). Then in order to cancel this contribution, the crossing disk is the corresponding anti-disk (corresponding to framing
1
) and when crossing that disk picks up a factor of
a^{2}
. After the crossing moment these two disks account for the whole partition function and we conclude that (
243
) holds.

8.6.2
The Hopf link partition function from bifurcations

Consider now instead the Hopf link, represented by two linked unknots where we take one of them very small. Consider shifted off conormals and corresponding basic annuli of the two components. Deform the complex structures so that the two annuli cross. After that time there are two unlinked annuli and one three holed sphere, with a framed unknot close to a standard unknot as boundary in
S^{3}
. We now stretch until all curve boundaries contract. Before the contraction, crossing disk and annuli moduli spaces that account for the extremal Hopf link (i.e., the curves with no
a
-powers in them) must appear. Since the extremal part of the (formal) partition function of a three holed sphere in Section
6.6
is an annulus partition function, Theorem
8.1
implies that all curves are accounted for by multiple covers of two disks and one annulus. Further, since the whole partition function is carried by the curves with boundary before the crossing the curves that cross are the anti-curves of the extremal Hopf. We conclude that the partition function is generated by four disks and two annuli that come in curve/anti-curve pairs. Finally, consider the extremal curves in the standard framing. It is straightforward to check that the configuration of curves in Figure
2
gives the partition function
1
at
a=1
: disks first cancel and then the pair of annulus/anti-annulus is a framed version of the standard canceling pair. If we knew that all holomorphic curves were multiple covers of the basic curves also after stretching, it would follow that the partition functions is generated as claimed.

Appendix A
The partition function of
L_{ll}
from the topological vertex

The partition function of HOMFLYPT polynomials for the Hopf link (
12
), which corresponds to the conormal-conormal filling
L_{ll}
, can also be obtained from the topological vertex.

More precisely, we have checked that (
12
), obtained as the unique normalized solution to
{\mathsf{A}}_{i}\cdot{\mathsf{Z}}_{L}=0
with
i=1,2,3
, agrees with the open topological string partition function of two toric branes on external legs in the resolved conifold

H_{\Gamma;\lambda,\mu}(a,q)=\frac{q^{-\kappa(\lambda)}}{(a^{2};q^{2};q^{2})_{\infty}}\sum_{\nu}C_{\lambda\mu^{t}\nu}(q)C_{00\nu^{t}}(q)\,(-a^{2})^{|\nu|}

(251)

where
C_{\lambda\mu\nu}(q)
is the topological vertex function
[
44
]
.
The normalization
q^{-\kappa(\lambda)}
corresponds to a choice of framing such that HOMFLYPT polynomials enjoy a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry
H_{\Gamma;\lambda,\mu}=H_{\Gamma;\mu,\lambda}
.

In a more general choice of framing, the partition function becomes

\begin{split}{\mathsf{Z}}_{ll}^{(f_{1},f_{2})}&=\Phi_{L_{1}}^{f_{1}}\otimes\Phi_{L_{2}}^{f_{2}}\,({\mathsf{Z}}_{\Gamma})\\
&=\sum_{\lambda,\mu}\left[(-1)^{|\lambda_{1}|}q^{\kappa(\lambda_{1})}\right]^{f_{1}}\left[(-1)^{|\lambda_{2}|}q^{\kappa(\lambda_{2})}\right]^{f_{2}}H_{\Gamma;\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}(a,q)\,{\mathsf{W}}_{\lambda_{1}}\otimes{\mathsf{W}}_{\lambda_{2}}\,,\end{split}

(252)

where the framing operators
\Phi_{L_{i}}^{f_{i}}
were defined in (
60
).
This obeys the framed skein valued recursion relations

{\mathsf{A}}_{i}^{(f_{1},f_{2})}\cdot{\mathsf{Z}}_{ll}^{(f_{1},f_{2})}=0\,,\quad\text{ for framings }(f_{1},f_{2})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\,,

(253)

defined by the operators (
162
)-(
163
)-(
164
),
after specializing
a_{\Lambda_{i}}=a_{L_{i}}
, and after introducing an additional rescaling of
P^{(\alpha)}_{i,j}
by appropriate powers of
a_{L_{\alpha}}^{-jf_{\alpha}}
as given by 4-chain intersections, see (
63
).
